===== .changeset/r29-python-adapters.md =====
---
"@olympus-ai/adapters": minor
"@olympus-ai/vault": minor
"@olympus-ai/api": minor
"@olympus-ai/readiness": minor
"@olympus-ai/conformance": minor
---

R29: a Python adapter set. pytest 8.4+ and 9, selected when `pyproject.toml` or a requirements file declares it and its version pinned by the lockfile, enumerates suites by pytest's own collection rules read statically and reads assertions, skip markers, and cases from a parse on the host; a config, conftest hook, or plugin that changes collection is refused, naming it. coverage.py's JSON report gives changed-line coverage. Python's config files are protected. `AdapterSet` names its dependency trees and its check image (A-R29-01); `DependencyInstaller.invocation` and `InstallRecord.invocation` become `steps` (A-R29-02), and uv (`uv.lock`) and pip (hashed `requirements.txt`) installs make a copied virtualenv and build nothing. Checks of a Python repository run in the image its install ran in, a task's writes to `.venv`, `site-packages`, or `__pycache__` are never carried, and readiness can clear a Python repository past L0.

===== packages/adapters/src/assertions.ts =====
diff --git a/packages/adapters/src/assertions.ts b/packages/adapters/src/assertions.ts
index c7bbfc2..b866bac 100644
--- a/packages/adapters/src/assertions.ts
+++ b/packages/adapters/src/assertions.ts
@@ -253,59 +253,70 @@ function key(a: Assertion): string {
 /**
  * Pairs `before` with `after` and reports what was lost. Identical
  * assertions pair first, wherever they moved to — line numbers and files are
  * not part of identity, so a moved test or a renamed file loses nothing. What
  * remains pairs by subject, same file first and then nearest line; a pair
  * that differs only by a wider tolerance is `toleranceWidened`, one whose new
  * form provably checks at least as much is dropped, and any other is
  * `weakened`. A `before` assertion left without a pair is `removed`.
  */
 export function compareAssertions(before: readonly Assertion[], after: readonly Assertion[]): AssertionDelta {
+  return pairAssertions(before, after, { atLeastAsStrong, negated });
+}
+
+/** What a language's comparison needs: which forms provably check at least as much, and which run a tolerance backwards. */
+export interface AssertionRules {
+  atLeastAsStrong(before: Assertion, after: Assertion): boolean;
+  negated(operator: string): boolean;
+}
+
+/** The pairing `compareAssertions` describes, over one language's `rules` (R29). */
+export function pairAssertions(before: readonly Assertion[], after: readonly Assertion[], rules: AssertionRules): AssertionDelta {
   const unmatched = [...after];
   const take = (index: number): void => {
     unmatched.splice(index, 1);
   };
   const remaining: Assertion[] = [];
   for (const b of before) {
     const sameFile = unmatched.findIndex((a) => a.file === b.file && key(a) === key(b));
     const anywhere = sameFile === -1 ? unmatched.findIndex((a) => key(a) === key(b)) : sameFile;
     if (anywhere === -1) remaining.push(b);
     else take(anywhere);
   }
 
   const delta: AssertionDelta = { weakened: [], removed: [], toleranceWidened: [] };
   for (const b of remaining) {
     const subject = b.args[0];
     let best = -1;
     for (let i = 0; i < unmatched.length; i++) {
       const a = unmatched[i];
       if (a === undefined || a.args[0] !== subject) continue;
       const current = unmatched[best];
       if (current === undefined) {
         best = i;
         continue;
       }
       const score = (x: Assertion): [number, number] => [x.file === b.file ? 0 : 1, Math.abs(x.line - b.line)];
       const [fileA, lineA] = score(a);
       const [fileC, lineC] = score(current);
       if (fileA < fileC || (fileA === fileC && lineA < lineC)) best = i;
     }
     const a = unmatched[best];
     if (a === undefined) {
       delta.removed.push(b);
       continue;
     }
     take(best);
     // An approximate matcher's arguments are the subject, the value, and then what sets the tolerance.
     const sameValue = a.operator === b.operator && a.args.slice(0, 2).join('\u0000') === b.args.slice(0, 2).join('\u0000');
     if (sameValue && a.tolerance !== undefined && b.tolerance !== undefined) {
       // Under a negation the tolerance runs the other way: `not.toBeCloseTo(v, 2)` becoming
       // `not.toBeCloseTo(v, 3)` narrows the band the value must stay out of, so more values pass.
       // What is reported either way is the change that lets more through.
-      const letsMoreThrough = negated(a.operator) ? a.tolerance < b.tolerance : a.tolerance > b.tolerance;
+      const letsMoreThrough = rules.negated(a.operator) ? a.tolerance < b.tolerance : a.tolerance > b.tolerance;
       if (letsMoreThrough) delta.toleranceWidened.push(a);
       continue;
     }
-    if (!atLeastAsStrong(b, a)) delta.weakened.push(a);
+    if (!rules.atLeastAsStrong(b, a)) delta.weakened.push(a);
   }
   return delta;
 }

===== packages/adapters/src/coverage.ts =====
diff --git a/packages/adapters/src/coverage.ts b/packages/adapters/src/coverage.ts
index 2031a76..e34fc57 100644
--- a/packages/adapters/src/coverage.ts

@@ -18,6 +18,7 @@ import { isConfigFile } from './config-files.js';
 import { diffTrees, exists, readRegularFile, SOURCE_FILE_CAP, toPosix, VCS_DIRECTORIES } from './files.js';
 import { changedLines, splitLines } from './line-diff.js';
 import { refuse } from './refusal.js';
+import { isDependencyTree } from './trees.js';
 import { parseModule } from './static.js';
 import type { CoverageAdapter, TestFrameworkAdapter } from './types.js';
 
@@ -111,70 +112,70 @@ function executableLines(text: string, fileName: string): Set<number> {
 export class IstanbulCoverageAdapter implements CoverageAdapter {
   readonly #options: IstanbulCoverageOptions;
 
   constructor(options: IstanbulCoverageOptions) {
     this.#options = options;
   }
 
   /** Every file entry in the report under `sourceRoot`, keyed by its path relative to it. */
   async #readReport(): Promise<Map<string, LineHits>> {
     const { report, sourceRoot } = this.#options;
     if (!(await exists(report))) refuse('missing-report', `no coverage report at ${report}`);
     let value: unknown;
     try {
       value = JSON.parse(await readRegularFile(report, REPORT_CAP));
     } catch (error) {
       if (error instanceof SyntaxError) refuse('malformed-report', `${report} is not JSON: ${error.message}`);
       throw error;
     }
     if (!isRecord(value)) refuse('malformed-report', `${report} is not a JSON object keyed by file`);
     // Both sides are normalised the same way, and by separator rather than by host: a report
     // written on Windows names its files with backslashes wherever it is read, and `toPosix`
     // converts the separator of the host doing the reading, which on Linux is already `/`.
     const prefix = `${sourceRoot.replaceAll('\\', '/').replace(/\/+$/, '')}/`;
     const files = new Map<string, LineHits>();
     const keys = Object.keys(value);
     for (const key of keys) {
       const normal = key.replaceAll('\\', '/');
       if (normal.startsWith(prefix)) files.set(normal.slice(prefix.length), lineHits(key, value[key]));
     }
     if (keys.length > 0 && files.size === 0) {
       refuse('malformed-report', `${report} names ${String(keys.length)} files and none under ${sourceRoot}, so it is not a report on this tree`);
     }
     return files;
   }
 
   async changedLineCoverage(base: string, head: string): Promise<number> {
     const report = await this.#readReport();
     const tests = new Set((await this.#options.tests.enumerateSuites(head)).map((file) => toPosix(file)));
     const headPosix = toPosix(head).replace(/\/+$/, '');
     const isSource = (path: string): boolean =>
       SOURCE.test(path) && !DECLARATION.test(path) && !isConfigFile(path) && !tests.has(`${headPosix}/${path}`);
 
     const changes = await diffTrees(base, head, isSource, {
-      skipDirectory: (name) => name === 'node_modules' || VCS_DIRECTORIES.has(name),
+      skipDirectory: (name) => isDependencyTree(name) || VCS_DIRECTORIES.has(name),
     });
     let total = 0;
     let covered = 0;
     for (const { path, change } of changes) {
       if (change === 'removed') continue;
       const after = await readRegularFile(join(head, path), SOURCE_FILE_CAP);
       const before = change === 'added' ? '' : await readRegularFile(join(base, path), SOURCE_FILE_CAP);
       const changed = changedLines(change === 'added' ? [] : splitLines(before), splitLines(after));
       const hits = report.get(path);
       // The report may add obligations and never remove one. Taking the denominator from the
       // report alone lets the file being judged decide what counts: an entry with an empty
       // statement map, or one an `istanbul ignore` comment thinned out, would leave nothing to
       // cover and read as covered in full. The repository's own exclusions are not honoured
       // (D-P8-09), and an ignore comment is one of them.
       const executable = executableLines(after, path);
       for (const line of hits?.keys() ?? []) executable.add(line);
       for (const line of changed) {
         if (!executable.has(line)) continue;
         total++;
         if ((hits?.get(line) ?? 0) > 0) covered++;
       }
     }
     // No executable line changed, so no changed line is uncovered.
     return total === 0 ? 1 : covered / total;
   }
 }

-export async function buildAdapterSet(root: string, options: AdapterSetOptions): Promise<TypeScriptAdapterSet> {
+/** pytest, or null with the reason recorded: not declared, not pinned, an unsupported major, or a config this package refuses (D-R29-04). */
+async function pythonTest(root: string, reasons: Map<Control, string>): Promise<TestFrameworkAdapter | null> {
+  try {
+    if (!(await declaresPytest(root))) {
+      reasons.set('test', 'pytest is not declared in pyproject.toml or a requirements file, and no other Python runner is supported');
+      return null;
+    }

+  } catch (error) {

+export async function buildAdapterSet(root: string, options: AdapterSetOptions): Promise<StackAdapterSet> {
   const reasons = new Map<Control, string>();
-  const manifest = await manifestOf(root, reasons);
-  const test = manifest === null ? null : await testAdapter(root, manifest, reasons);
-  const hasManifest = manifest !== null;
+  const nodeReasons = new Map<Control, string>();
+  const pythonReasons = new Map<Control, string>();
+  const manifest = await manifestOf(root, nodeReasons);
+  const nodeTest = manifest === null ? null : await testAdapter(root, manifest, nodeReasons);
+  const python = await isPythonProject(root);
+  const pythonTestAdapter = python ? await pythonTest(root, pythonReasons) : null;

+  let test: TestFrameworkAdapter | null = null;
+  let stack: 'node' | 'python' | null = null;
+  if (nodeTest !== null && pythonTestAdapter !== null) {
+    reasons.set('test', `both ${nodeTest.stack} and ${pythonTestAdapter.stack} are declared, and which suite is authoritative is not something to guess`);
+  } else if (pythonTestAdapter !== null) {

+  } else if (python && manifest !== null) {

===== packages/adapters/src/python/config.ts =====
/**
 * pytest's configuration, read the way pytest reads it and never run
 * (D-R29-04, D-R29-06): which files configure it, in its own order for each
 * supported major (`_pytest/config/findpaths.py`, 8.4.2 and 9.2.0), the
 * settings that decide which files and names are tests, and the version the
 * lockfile pins. A setting that changes collection in a way this package
 * does not model is refused, naming it, as P8 refuses a computed config.
 */
import { readdir } from 'node:fs/promises';
import { join } from 'node:path';
import { parse as parseToml } from 'smol-toml';
import { exists, readRegularFile, SOURCE_FILE_CAP } from '../files.js';
import { describe, refuse } from '../refusal.js';
import { parseIni, splitArgs } from './ini.js';

export type PytestMajor = 8 | 9;

/** The settings that decide what pytest collects, as one tree's config sets them. */
export interface PytestRules {
  readonly major: PytestMajor;
  /** The root file the settings came from, or null for pytest's defaults. */
  readonly configFile: string | null;
  readonly testpaths: readonly string[];
  readonly pythonFiles: readonly string[];
  readonly pythonClasses: readonly string[];
  readonly pythonFunctions: readonly string[];
  readonly norecursedirs: readonly string[];
  /** pytest 9 shortens a string argvalue over 100 characters to `<argname><index>` unless this is `legacy`. */
  readonly longStringIds: 'short' | 'legacy';
}

/** pytest's defaults, identical in 8.4.2 and 9.2.0 (`_pytest/main.py`, `_pytest/python.py`). */
export const PYTEST_DEFAULTS = {
  testpaths: [] as readonly string[],
  pythonFiles: ['test_*.py', '*_test.py'] as readonly string[],
  pythonClasses: ['Test'] as readonly string[],
  pythonFunctions: ['test'] as readonly string[],
  norecursedirs: ['*.egg', '.*', '_darcs', 'build', 'CVS', 'dist', 'node_modules', 'venv', '{arch}'] as readonly string[],
} as const;

/** The files each major reads its config from, in the order it tries them in one directory (`CONFIG_LOADERS_BY_NAME`). */
export const PYTEST_CONFIG_NAMES: Readonly<Record<PytestMajor, readonly string[]>> = {
  8: ['pytest.ini', '.pytest.ini', 'pyproject.toml', 'tox.ini', 'setup.cfg'],
  9: ['pytest.toml', '.pytest.toml', 'pytest.ini', '.pytest.ini', 'pyproject.toml', 'tox.ini', 'setup.cfg'],
};

type Value = string | readonly string[];
type Settings = ReadonlyMap<string, Value>;

function isRecord(value: unknown): value is Record<string, unknown> {
  return typeof value === 'object' && value !== null && !Array.isArray(value) && !(value instanceof Date);
}

async function readToml(path: string): Promise<Record<string, unknown>> {
  try {
    return parseToml(await readRegularFile(path, SOURCE_FILE_CAP));
  } catch (error) {
    return refuse('unparseable', `${path} is not TOML pytest can read: ${describe(error)}`);
  }
}

/** A TOML table's settings, each a string or a list of strings; anything else is a value this package does not read. */
function tomlSettings(table: Record<string, unknown>, label: string): Settings {
  const out = new Map<string, Value>();
  for (const [key, value] of Object.entries(table)) {
    if (typeof value === 'string') out.set(key, value);
    else if (Array.isArray(value) && value.every((v): v is string => typeof v === 'string')) out.set(key, value);
    else if (typeof value === 'boolean' || typeof value === 'number') out.set(key, String(value));
    else refuse('unresolvable-config', `${label} sets ${key} to a value that is neither a string nor a list of strings`);
  }
  return out;
}

/**
 * The settings `file` holds for pytest, or null when pytest would pass over
 * it. Each loader is the major's own: a dedicated file always configures
 * pytest, a shared one only with its pytest section.
 */
export async function loadConfig(path: string, name: string, major: PytestMajor): Promise<Settings | null> {
  if (name === 'pytest.ini' || name === '.pytest.ini' || name === 'tox.ini' || name === 'setup.cfg') {
    const ini = parseIni(await readRegularFile(path, SOURCE_FILE_CAP), path);
    if (name === 'setup.cfg') {
      if (ini.has('pytest') && !ini.has('tool:pytest')) refuse('unresolvable-config', `${path} has a [pytest] section, which pytest refuses in setup.cfg`);
      return ini.get('tool:pytest') ?? null;
    }
    const section = ini.get('pytest');
    if (section !== undefined) return section;
    return name === 'tox.ini' ? null : new Map();
  }
  const toml = await readToml(path);
  if (name === 'pytest.toml' || name === '.pytest.toml') {
    if (isRecord(toml.pytest)) return tomlSettings(toml.pytest, `${path} [pytest]`);
    const loose = Object.keys(toml).filter((k) => !isRecord(toml[k]));
    if (loose.length > 0) refuse('unresolvable-config', `${path} sets ${loose.join(', ')} outside a [pytest] table, which pytest refuses`);
    return new Map();
  }
  // pyproject.toml
  const tool = isRecord(toml.tool) ? toml.tool : {};
  const pytest = isRecord(tool.pytest) ? tool.pytest : undefined;
  if (pytest === undefined) return null;
  const ini = isRecord(pytest.ini_options) ? pytest.ini_options : undefined;
  const native = Object.fromEntries(Object.entries(pytest).filter(([k]) => k !== 'ini_options'));
  if (major === 8) return ini === undefined ? null : tomlSettings(ini, `${path} [tool.pytest.ini_options]`);
  if (ini !== undefined && Object.keys(native).length > 0) refuse('unresolvable-config', `${path} sets both [tool.pytest] and [tool.pytest.ini_options], which pytest refuses`);
  if (ini !== undefined) return tomlSettings(ini, `${path} [tool.pytest.ini_options]`);
  return Object.keys(native).length > 0 ? tomlSettings(native, `${path} [tool.pytest]`) : null;
}

/** The root's config file and its settings, by the major's order; pytest's defaults when the root holds none. */
export async function rootConfig(root: string, major: PytestMajor): Promise<{ readonly file: string | null; readonly settings: Settings }> {
  for (const name of PYTEST_CONFIG_NAMES[major]) {
    const path = join(root, name);
    if (!(await exists(path))) continue;
    const settings = await loadConfig(path, name, major);
    if (settings !== null) return { file: name, settings };
  }
  return { file: null, settings: new Map() };
}

function argsOf(settings: Settings, key: string, label: string): string[] | undefined {
  const value = settings.get(key);
  if (value === undefined) return undefined;
  return typeof value === 'string' ? splitArgs(value, `${label} ${key}`) : [...value];
}

/** addopts that change only what is printed or how a failure stops the run, never what is collected (D-R29-06). */
const OUTPUT_FLAGS = new Set([
  '-q', '-qq', '-qqq', '--quiet', '-v', '-vv', '-vvv', '--verbose', '-l', '--showlocals', '-s', '--no-header', '--no-summary',
  '--strict-markers', '--strict-config', '--strict', '-x', '--exitfirst',
]);
const OUTPUT_PREFIXES = ['--tb=', '--color=', '--durations=', '--durations-min=', '--maxfail=', '--capture=', '--import-mode=', '--log-level=', '--log-cli-level='];

function checkAddopts(tokens: readonly string[], label: string): void {
  for (let i = 0; i < tokens.length; i++) {
    const token = tokens[i] ?? '';
    if (OUTPUT_FLAGS.has(token) || OUTPUT_PREFIXES.some((p) => token.startsWith(p)) || /^-r[fEsxXpPaAN]+$/u.test(token)) continue;
    if (token === '-W' && i + 1 < tokens.length) {
      i++;
      continue;
    }
    if (/^-W.+/u.test(token) || token.startsWith('--pythonwarnings=')) continue;
    refuse('unsupported-feature', `${label} addopts passes ${token}, which may change what pytest collects and is not modelled`);
  }
}

/** The settings that decide collection, read from the root's config, with every one that changes it unmodelled refused. */
export async function pytestRules(root: string, major: PytestMajor): Promise<PytestRules> {
  const { file, settings } = await rootConfig(root, major);
  const label = file ?? 'the pytest defaults';
  for (const [key, value] of settings) {
    const text = typeof value === 'string' ? value : value.join(' ');
    // iniconfig strips an inline comment in some versions and not others, so a value holding one is read two ways.
    if (file !== null && !file.endsWith('.toml') && /[#;]/u.test(text)) {
      refuse('unresolvable-config', `${label} ${key} holds a comment character, which iniconfig versions read differently`);
    }
  }
  checkAddopts(argsOf(settings, 'addopts', label) ?? [], label);
  const plugins = argsOf(settings, 'required_plugins', label) ?? [];
  if (plugins.length > 0) refuse('unsupported-feature', `${label} requires plugins (${plugins.join(', ')}), and a plugin that changes collection is not modelled`);
  const strategy = settings.get('parametrize_long_str_id_strategy');
  if (strategy !== undefined && strategy !== 'short' && strategy !== 'legacy') refuse('unresolvable-config', `${label} parametrize_long_str_id_strategy is ${String(strategy)}`);
  return {
    major,
    configFile: file,
    testpaths: argsOf(settings, 'testpaths', label) ?? PYTEST_DEFAULTS.testpaths,
    pythonFiles: argsOf(settings, 'python_files', label) ?? PYTEST_DEFAULTS.pythonFiles,
    pythonClasses: argsOf(settings, 'python_classes', label) ?? PYTEST_DEFAULTS.pythonClasses,
    pythonFunctions: argsOf(settings, 'python_functions', label) ?? PYTEST_DEFAULTS.pythonFunctions,
    norecursedirs: argsOf(settings, 'norecursedirs', label) ?? PYTEST_DEFAULTS.norecursedirs,
    longStringIds: strategy === 'legacy' || major === 8 ? 'legacy' : 'short',
  };
}

// ---- declaration and version ----------------------------------------------

/** A PEP 508 requirement's name, normalised (PEP 503), or undefined for a line that names none. */
export function requirementName(spec: string): string | undefined {
  const match = /^\s*([A-Za-z0-9](?:[A-Za-z0-9._-]*[A-Za-z0-9])?)/u.exec(spec);
  return match?.[1]?.toLowerCase().replace(/[-_.]+/gu, '-');
}

/** The requirement strings `pyproject.toml` declares, in every place uv and PEP 621 and 735 put one. */
function declaredRequirements(toml: Record<string, unknown>): string[] {
  const out: string[] = [];
  const strings = (value: unknown): void => {
    if (Array.isArray(value)) for (const v of value) if (typeof v === 'string') out.push(v);
  };
  const project = isRecord(toml.project) ? toml.project : {};
  strings(project.dependencies);
  if (isRecord(project['optional-dependencies'])) for (const v of Object.values(project['optional-dependencies'])) strings(v);
  if (isRecord(toml['dependency-groups'])) for (const v of Object.values(toml['dependency-groups'])) strings(v);
  const tool = isRecord(toml.tool) ? toml.tool : {};
  const uv = isRecord(tool.uv) ? tool.uv : {};
  strings(uv['dev-dependencies']);
  return out;
}

/** The root's requirements files, `requirements.txt` and its `requirements-dev.txt`-style siblings; a `requirements/` directory is not read. */
async function requirementsFiles(root: string): Promise<string[]> {
  const names = (await readdir(root, { withFileTypes: true })).filter((e) => e.isFile() && /^requirements[\w.-]*\.txt$/u.test(e.name)).map((e) => e.name);
  return names.sort();
}

/** The requirement lines of a requirements file, continuations joined and comments dropped. */
export function requirementLines(text: string): Array<{ readonly line: number; readonly text: string }> {
  const out: Array<{ line: number; text: string }> = [];
  let pending: { line: number; text: string } | null = null;
  const lines = text.split(/\r\n|\n|\r/u);
  for (const [index, raw] of lines.entries()) {
    const hash = raw.search(/(^|\s)#/u);
    const line = (hash === -1 ? raw : raw.slice(0, hash)).trimEnd();
    const continues = line.endsWith('\\');
    const body = continues ? line.slice(0, -1) : line;
    if (pending === null) pending = { line: index + 1, text: body };
    else pending.text += ` ${body}`;
    if (continues) continue;
    if (pending.text.trim() !== '') out.push({ line: pending.line, text: pending.text.trim() });
    pending = null;
  }
  if (pending !== null && pending.text.trim() !== '') out.push({ line: pending.line, text: pending.text.trim() });
  return out;
}

/** Whether the repository declares pytest: in `pyproject.toml` or a root requirements file (D-R29-04). */
export async function declaresPytest(root: string): Promise<boolean> {
  if (await exists(join(root, 'pyproject.toml'))) {
    if (declaredRequirements(await readToml(join(root, 'pyproject.toml'))).some((r) => requirementName(r) === 'pytest')) return true;
  }
  for (const name of await requirementsFiles(root)) {
    const lines = requirementLines(await readRegularFile(join(root, name), SOURCE_FILE_CAP));
    if (lines.some((l) => requirementName(l.text) === 'pytest')) return true;
  }
  return false;
}

/** Whether the root holds a Python project: a `pyproject.toml` or a root requirements file. */
export async function isPythonProject(root: string): Promise<boolean> {
  return (await exists(join(root, 'pyproject.toml'))) || (await requirementsFiles(root)).length > 0;
}

/** The pytest version the lockfile pins: `uv.lock`'s package, or `requirements.txt`'s `==` (D-R29-04). */
export async function pinnedPytest(root: string): Promise<string> {
  const pins: Array<{ readonly from: string; readonly version: string }> = [];
  if (await exists(join(root, 'uv.lock'))) {
    const lock = await readToml(join(root, 'uv.lock'));
    const packages = Array.isArray(lock.package) ? lock.package : [];
    for (const p of packages) {
      if (isRecord(p) && typeof p.name === 'string' && requirementName(p.name) === 'pytest' && typeof p.version === 'string') pins.push({ from: 'uv.lock', version: p.version });
    }
  }
  if (await exists(join(root, 'requirements.txt'))) {
    for (const l of requirementLines(await readRegularFile(join(root, 'requirements.txt'), SOURCE_FILE_CAP))) {
      if (requirementName(l.text) !== 'pytest') continue;
      const match = /^[A-Za-z0-9._-]+\s*==\s*([0-9][0-9A-Za-z.+!-]*)(?:\s|;|$)/u.exec(l.text);
      if (match?.[1] === undefined) refuse('unsupported-version', `requirements.txt line ${String(l.line)} does not pin pytest with ==`);
      pins.push({ from: 'requirements.txt', version: match[1] });
    }
  }
  const versions = [...new Set(pins.map((p) => p.version))];
  const [only] = versions;
  if (only === undefined) return refuse('unsupported-version', 'pytest is declared and no lockfile pins its version (uv.lock or a hashed requirements.txt), so which pytest runs is not known');
  if (versions.length > 1) refuse('unsupported-version', `pytest is pinned at several versions (${pins.map((p) => `${p.version} in ${p.from}`).join(', ')})`);
  return only;
}

/** The major a pinned version selects: 8 from 8.4 on, or 9 (D-R29-04). Any other is refused. */
export function pytestMajor(version: string): PytestMajor {
  const match = /^(\d+)\.(\d+)/u.exec(version);
  const major = Number(match?.[1]);
  const minor = Number(match?.[2]);
  if (major === 9) return 9;
  if (major === 8 && minor >= 4) return 8;
  return refuse('unsupported-version', `pytest ${version} is pinned, and this package encodes the collection rules of pytest 8.4 and later and of pytest 9`);
}

===== packages/adapters/src/python/coverage.ts =====
/**
 * Changed-line coverage from coverage.py's JSON report (`coverage json`,
 * format 3): per file, `executed_lines` and `missing_lines`. The report is
 * written by a check in the sandbox; this adapter reads it on the host and
 * runs nothing.
 *
 * A changed line is an obligation when a statement starts on it, by
 * coverage.py's own measure (D-R29-02): each statement's first line,
 * decorators and the `def` both, `elif`, `except`, and `case` lines, and a
 * module's or class's docstring; not `global`, `nonlocal`, a function's
 * docstring, `else:`, or `finally:`. The report may add obligations and
 * never remove one, so a `# pragma: no cover` thins nothing (D-P8-09).
 */
import { join } from 'node:path';
import type { SyntaxNode } from '@lezer/common';
import { isConfigFile } from '../config-files.js';
import { diffTrees, exists, readRegularFile, SOURCE_FILE_CAP, toPosix, VCS_DIRECTORIES } from '../files.js';
import { changedLines, splitLines } from '../line-diff.js';
import { refuse } from '../refusal.js';
import { isDependencyTree } from '../trees.js';
import type { CoverageAdapter, TestFrameworkAdapter } from '../types.js';
import { REPORT_CAP } from '../coverage.js';
import { childrenOf, lineOf, parsePython, statementsOf, type PythonModule } from './parse.js';

export interface CoveragePyOptions {
  /** Absolute host path of the JSON report the coverage check wrote. */
  readonly report: string;
  /** The tree's root as the process that wrote the report saw it, e.g. `/workspace`. */
  readonly sourceRoot: string;
  /** Used to exclude test files from the lines that must be covered. */
  readonly tests: TestFrameworkAdapter;
}

interface FileLines { readonly executed: ReadonlySet<number>; readonly measured: ReadonlySet<number> }

function isRecord(value: unknown): value is Record<string, unknown> {
  return typeof value === 'object' && value !== null && !Array.isArray(value);
}

function lines(key: string, field: string, value: unknown): number[] {
  if (!Array.isArray(value) || !value.every((n) => Number.isInteger(n) && (n as number) > 0)) {
    refuse('malformed-report', `the entry for ${key} has no ${field} list of line numbers`);
  }
  return value as number[];
}

const DOCSTRING_BODY = new Set(['FunctionDefinition']);

/** The lines coverage.py counts as statements, read from the source. */
export function executablePythonLines(module: PythonModule): Set<number> {
  const out = new Set<number>();
  const add = (node: SyntaxNode): void => { out.add(lineOf(module, node.from)); };
  module.tree.iterate({
    enter: (ref) => {
      const node = ref.node;
      const name = node.name;
      if (name === 'Decorator' || name === 'MatchClause') add(node);
      else if (name === 'elif' || name === 'except') add(node);
      else if (name === 'FunctionDefinition' || name === 'ClassDefinition') {
        const keyword = childrenOf(node).find((c) => c.name === 'def' || c.name === 'class' || c.name === 'async');
        add(keyword ?? node);
      } else if (name.endsWith('Statement') && name !== 'DecoratedStatement' && name !== 'ScopeStatement') {
        if (name === 'ExpressionStatement' && isFunctionDocstring(node)) return;
        add(node);
      }
    },
  });
  return out;
}

/** A string alone as the first statement of a function's body: its docstring, which carries no line of its own. */
function isFunctionDocstring(node: SyntaxNode): boolean {
  const [only] = childrenOf(node);
  if (only?.name !== 'String' && only?.name !== 'ContinuedString') return false;
  const body = node.parent;
  if (body?.name !== 'Body' || body.parent === null || !DOCSTRING_BODY.has(body.parent.name)) return false;
  return statementsOf(body)[0]?.from === node.from;
}

export class CoveragePyAdapter implements CoverageAdapter {
  readonly #options: CoveragePyOptions;

  constructor(options: CoveragePyOptions) {
    this.#options = options;
  }

  async #readReport(): Promise<Map<string, FileLines>> {
    const { report, sourceRoot } = this.#options;
    if (!(await exists(report))) refuse('missing-report', `no coverage report at ${report}`);
    let value: unknown;
    try {
      value = JSON.parse(await readRegularFile(report, REPORT_CAP));
    } catch (error) {
      if (error instanceof SyntaxError) refuse('malformed-report', `${report} is not JSON: ${error.message}`);
      throw error;
    }
    if (!isRecord(value) || !isRecord(value.files)) refuse('malformed-report', `${report} is not a coverage.py JSON report: it has no files object`);
    const prefix = `${sourceRoot.replaceAll('\\', '/').replace(/\/+$/u, '')}/`;
    const out = new Map<string, FileLines>();
    const keys = Object.keys(value.files);
    for (const key of keys) {
      const entry = value.files[key];
      if (!isRecord(entry)) refuse('malformed-report', `the entry for ${key} is not an object`);
      const executed = lines(key, 'executed_lines', entry.executed_lines);
      const missing = lines(key, 'missing_lines', entry.missing_lines);
      const normal = key.replaceAll('\\', '/');
      // coverage.py names a file under the directory it ran in relative to it, and any other absolutely.
      const path = normal.startsWith(prefix) ? normal.slice(prefix.length) : normal.startsWith('/') ? null : normal.replace(/^\.\//u, '');
      if (path !== null) out.set(path, { executed: new Set(executed), measured: new Set([...executed, ...missing]) });
    }
    if (keys.length > 0 && out.size === 0) refuse('malformed-report', `${report} names ${String(keys.length)} files and none under ${sourceRoot}, so it is not a report on this tree`);
    return out;
  }

  async changedLineCoverage(base: string, head: string): Promise<number> {
    const report = await this.#readReport();
    const tests = new Set((await this.#options.tests.enumerateSuites(head)).map((file) => toPosix(file)));
    const headPosix = toPosix(head).replace(/\/+$/u, '');
    const isSource = (path: string): boolean => path.endsWith('.py') && !isConfigFile(path) && !tests.has(`${headPosix}/${path}`);
    const changes = await diffTrees(base, head, isSource, { skipDirectory: (name) => isDependencyTree(name) || VCS_DIRECTORIES.has(name) });
    let total = 0;
    let covered = 0;
    for (const { path, change } of changes) {
      if (change === 'removed') continue;
      const after = await readRegularFile(join(head, path), SOURCE_FILE_CAP);
      const before = change === 'added' ? '' : await readRegularFile(join(base, path), SOURCE_FILE_CAP);
      const changed = changedLines(change === 'added' ? [] : splitLines(before), splitLines(after));
      const entry = report.get(path);
      const executable = executablePythonLines(parsePython(after, path));
      for (const line of entry?.measured ?? []) executable.add(line);
      for (const line of changed) {
        if (!executable.has(line)) continue;
        total++;
        if (entry?.executed.has(line) === true) covered++;
      }
    }
    return total === 0 ? 1 : covered / total;
  }
}

===== packages/adapters/src/python/discovery.ts =====
/**
 * The suites pytest collects, enumerated on the host from files alone by
 * pytest's own rules (`_pytest/main.py`, `_pytest/python.py`, 8.4.2 and
 * 9.2.0): `testpaths`, globbed from the root when no path is given, else the
 * root; every directory entered but `__pycache__`, a virtualenv, and one
 * `norecursedirs` matches; every `.py` file `python_files` matches; and a
 * named file collected whatever its name. Before any of that, every conftest
 * and every config file below the root is checked for what would change
 * collection without the root's settings saying so (D-R29-06).
 */
import type { SyntaxNode } from '@lezer/common';
import { lstat, readdir } from 'node:fs/promises';
import { join, posix, relative, resolve } from 'node:path';
import { glob } from 'tinyglobby';
import { exists, toPosix, VCS_DIRECTORIES, walkTree } from '../files.js';
import { refuse } from '../refusal.js';
import { isDependencyTree } from '../trees.js';
import { loadConfig, pinnedPytest, PYTEST_CONFIG_NAMES, pytestMajor, pytestRules, rootConfig, type PytestMajor, type PytestRules } from './config.js';
import { fnmatch } from './ini.js';
import { argumentsOf, callOf, childrenOf, readPython, statementsOf, textOf, type PythonModule } from './parse.js';

/** Where the sandbox mounts the tree: the path pytest's absolute-path patterns are matched against. */
const MOUNT = '/workspace';

/** `fnmatch_ex`: a pattern with no `/` matches the name, one with a `/` the whole path, relative patterns anchored anywhere. */
export function fnmatchEx(pattern: string, path: string): boolean {
  if (!pattern.includes('/')) return fnmatch(posix.basename(path), pattern);
  return fnmatch(path, posix.isAbsolute(pattern) ? pattern : `*/${pattern}`);
}

/** Hook names whose definition in a conftest changes what is collected (D-R29-06). */
const COLLECTION_HOOK = /^pytest_(?:collect|ignore_collect|pycollect_|generate_tests|collection|make_collect_report|load_initial_conftests|cmdline_|sessionstart|plugin_registered|addhooks)/u;
const COLLECTION_VARIABLES = new Set(['pytest_plugins', 'collect_ignore', 'collect_ignore_glob']);

/** A `pytest_configure` that only registers markers: every statement `config.addinivalue_line("markers", ...)`, a docstring, or `pass`. */
function onlyRegistersMarkers(module: PythonModule, def: SyntaxNode): boolean {
  const params = childrenOf(def).find((c) => c.name === 'ParamList');
  const first = params === undefined ? undefined : childrenOf(params).find((c) => c.name === 'VariableName');
  const body = childrenOf(def).find((c) => c.name === 'Body');
  if (first === undefined || body === undefined) return false;
  const config = textOf(module, first);
  return statementsOf(body).every((s) => {
    if (s.name === 'PassStatement') return true;
    if (s.name !== 'ExpressionStatement') return false;
    const [expr] = childrenOf(s);
    if (expr?.name === 'String') return true;
    const call = expr === undefined ? undefined : callOf(module, expr);
    if (call?.name !== `${config}.addinivalue_line`) return false;
    const args = argumentsOf(module, call.args);
    const kind = args?.positional[0];
    return kind !== undefined && /^["']markers["']$/u.test(textOf(module, kind));
  });
}

/** Refuses a conftest that changes collection: a plugin it loads, a path it ignores, or a collection hook (D-R29-06). */
export async function checkConftest(file: string, label: string): Promise<void> {
  const module = await readPython(file);
  for (const statement of statementsOf(module.tree.topNode)) {
    const def = statement.name === 'DecoratedStatement' ? childrenOf(statement).find((c) => c.name === 'FunctionDefinition') : statement;
    if (def?.name === 'FunctionDefinition') {
      const name = childrenOf(def).find((c) => c.name === 'VariableName');
      const hook = name === undefined ? '' : textOf(module, name);
      if (hook === 'pytest_configure' && !onlyRegistersMarkers(module, def)) {
        refuse('unsupported-feature', `${label} defines pytest_configure, which can change what pytest collects and is not read statically`);
      }
      if (COLLECTION_HOOK.test(hook)) refuse('unsupported-feature', `${label} defines ${hook}, which changes what pytest collects`);
    }
    if (statement.name === 'AssignStatement' || statement.name === 'UpdateStatement') {
      for (const target of childrenOf(statement)) {
        if (target.name === 'AssignOp' || target.name === 'UpdateOp') break;
        if (target.name === 'VariableName' && COLLECTION_VARIABLES.has(textOf(module, target))) {
          refuse('unsupported-feature', `${label} sets ${textOf(module, target)}, which changes what pytest collects`);
        }
      }
    }
  }
}

function skipped(name: string): boolean {
  return VCS_DIRECTORIES.has(name) || isDependencyTree(name);
}

/**
 * The checks over the whole tree: every conftest, and every config file below
 * the root that pytest would read for a test beneath it, since it finds its
 * config by walking up from each path it is given (`locate_config`).
 */
async function checkTree(root: string, major: PytestMajor, rootHasConfig: boolean): Promise<void> {
  const names = new Set(PYTEST_CONFIG_NAMES[major]);
  const entries = await walkTree(root, { skipDirectory: skipped });
  for (const [path, entry] of [...entries].sort(([a], [b]) => a.localeCompare(b))) {
    const name = posix.basename(path);
    if (entry.kind !== 'file') continue;
    if (name === 'conftest.py') await checkConftest(entry.absolute, path);
    if (!path.includes('/') || !names.has(name)) continue;
    const settings = await loadConfig(entry.absolute, name, major);
    if (settings !== null) refuse('unsupported-feature', `${path} configures pytest below the root, so a test beneath it is collected by other settings`);
    if (name === 'pyproject.toml' && !rootHasConfig) refuse('unsupported-feature', `${path} would make its directory pytest's rootdir for a test beneath it, as the root holds no pytest config`);
  }
}

export interface PytestDiscovery {
  readonly version: string;
  readonly rules: PytestRules;
}

/** The pinned version, its major's rules from the root's config, and the tree checked for what would change them. */
export async function pytestDiscovery(root: string): Promise<PytestDiscovery> {
  const version = await pinnedPytest(root);
  const major = pytestMajor(version);
  const rules = await pytestRules(root, major);
  await checkTree(root, major, (await rootConfig(root, major)).file !== null);
  return { version, rules };
}

/** A virtualenv's root, which pytest never enters (`_in_venv`). */
async function isVenv(dir: string): Promise<boolean> {
  return (await exists(join(dir, 'pyvenv.cfg'))) || (await exists(join(dir, 'conda-meta', 'history')));
}

/** The paths `testpaths` names, globbed from the root as pytest globs them, or the root when it names none that exist. */
async function startPaths(root: string, rules: PytestRules): Promise<string[]> {
  const found: string[] = [];
  for (const pattern of rules.testpaths) {
    if (/[{}]/u.test(pattern) || posix.isAbsolute(pattern) || pattern.split('/').includes('..')) {
      refuse('unresolvable-config', `${rules.configFile ?? 'the config'} testpaths entry ${pattern} is not a pattern under the root this package reads`);
    }
    const matched = await glob(pattern, { cwd: root, dot: false, onlyFiles: false, expandDirectories: false, followSymbolicLinks: false });
    found.push(...matched.map((m) => m.replace(/\/+$/u, '')).sort());
  }
  return found.length === 0 ? [''] : [...new Set(found)];
}

/** Every suite pytest collects from the root with no path given, absolute and sorted. */
export async function pytestSuites(root: string): Promise<string[]> {
  const { rules } = await pytestDiscovery(root);
  const top = resolve(root);
  const out = new Set<string>();
  const collects = (rel: string): boolean => rel.endsWith('.py') && rules.pythonFiles.some((p) => fnmatchEx(p, `${MOUNT}/${rel}`));
  const visit = async (rel: string): Promise<void> => {
    const dir = join(top, ...rel.split('/').filter(Boolean));
    const children = (await readdir(dir, { withFileTypes: true })).sort((a, b) => (a.name < b.name ? -1 : a.name > b.name ? 1 : 0));
    for (const child of children) {
      const path = rel === '' ? child.name : `${rel}/${child.name}`;
      if (child.isSymbolicLink()) {
        if (skipped(child.name) || child.name === '__pycache__') continue;
        refuse('unsafe-path', `${path} is a symbolic link where pytest collects, and pytest follows it`);
      }
      if (child.isDirectory()) {
        if (child.name === '__pycache__' || skipped(child.name)) continue;
        if (await isVenv(join(dir, child.name))) continue;
        if (rules.norecursedirs.some((p) => fnmatchEx(p, `${MOUNT}/${path}`))) continue;
        await visit(path);
      } else if (child.isFile() && collects(path)) out.add(join(top, ...path.split('/')));
    }
  };
  for (const start of await startPaths(top, rules)) {
    const absolute = join(top, ...start.split('/').filter(Boolean));
    const facts = await lstat(absolute);
    if (facts.isSymbolicLink()) refuse('unsafe-path', `testpaths names ${start}, a symbolic link`);
    // A path pytest is given is collected whatever python_files says (`isinitpath`).
    if (facts.isFile()) {
      if (start.endsWith('.py')) out.add(absolute);
    } else if (facts.isDirectory()) await visit(start);
  }
  return [...out].sort();
}

/**
 * The suites `pytest <tests…>` runs from the root: each named file that is a
 * regular `.py` file in the tree, collected whatever its name, since a path
 * pytest is given is not matched against `python_files`.
 */
export async function pytestSelection(root: string, tests: readonly string[]): Promise<string[]> {
  await pytestDiscovery(root);
  const top = resolve(root);
  const out = new Set<string>();
  for (const test of tests) {
    const rel = toPosix(relative(top, resolve(top, test)));
    if (rel.startsWith('..') || posix.isAbsolute(rel) || !rel.endsWith('.py')) continue;
    const facts = await lstat(join(top, ...rel.split('/'))).catch(() => null);
    if (facts?.isFile() === true) out.add(rel);
  }
  return [...out].sort();
}

===== packages/adapters/src/python/framework.ts =====
/**
 * `TestFrameworkAdapter` for pytest 8.4+ and 9 (R29). Suites are enumerated
 * by pytest's rules from the tree's own config; assertions, skip markers,
 * and cases from the parse. The runner is the interpreter the runtime's
 * install copied into `.venv`, starting pytest with no plugin autoloaded, no
 * doctest collection, and no cache written into a read-only tree
 * (D-R29-06).
 */
import { readdir, stat } from 'node:fs/promises';
import { join } from 'node:path';
import type { Assertion, AssertionDelta, TestFrameworkAdapter } from '../types.js';
import type { PytestMajor, PytestRules } from './config.js';
import { pytestSelection, pytestSuites } from './discovery.js';
import { readPython } from './parse.js';
import { comparePythonAssertions, extractAssertions, readTestFile, spellPython } from './tests.js';

/** A named test as a runner argument: `./`-led, so a file named `-x.py` is never an option. */
function asArgument(test: string): string {
  return `./${test}`;
}

export class PytestAdapter implements TestFrameworkAdapter {
  readonly stack: string;
  readonly runner = '.venv/bin/python';
  readonly #rules: PytestRules;

  /** `rules` are the tree's own, read when its set was built; cases are named by its `python_classes` and `python_functions`. */
  constructor(rules: PytestRules) {
    this.#rules = rules;
    this.stack = `pytest@${String(rules.major satisfies PytestMajor)}`;
  }

  invocation(tests: readonly string[] | null): readonly [string, ...string[]] {
    return [this.runner, '-m', 'pytest', '--disable-plugin-autoload', '-p', 'no:cacheprovider', '-p', 'no:doctest', ...(tests ?? []).map(asArgument)];
  }

  selectSuites(dir: string, tests: readonly string[]): Promise<string[]> {
    return pytestSelection(dir, tests);
  }

  enumerateSuites(dir: string): Promise<string[]> {
    return pytestSuites(dir);
  }

  async parseAssertions(file: string): Promise<Assertion[]> {
    return extractAssertions(await readPython(file));
  }

  compareAssertions(before: Assertion[], after: Assertion[]): AssertionDelta {
    return comparePythonAssertions(before, after);
  }

  async detectSkipMarkers(file: string): Promise<string[]> {
    return readTestFile(await readPython(file), this.#rules).skips;
  }

  async enumerateCases(file: string): Promise<string[]> {
    return readTestFile(await readPython(file), this.#rules).cases;
  }

  /** The interpreter the install copied, and pytest in its `site-packages`; either missing and the check cannot start. */
  async missingRunner(tree: string): Promise<string | null> {
    const python = await stat(join(tree, '.venv', 'bin', 'python')).catch(() => null);
    if (python?.isFile() !== true) return `${this.stack}'s interpreter is not installed at ${this.runner}, so the check cannot start`;
    const lib = join(tree, '.venv', 'lib');
    const versions = (await readdir(lib, { withFileTypes: true }).catch(() => [])).filter((e) => e.isDirectory() && /^python3\.\d+$/u.test(e.name));
    for (const v of versions) {
      const init = await stat(join(lib, v.name, 'site-packages', 'pytest', '__init__.py')).catch(() => null);
      if (init?.isFile() === true) return null;
    }
    return `${this.stack} is not installed in .venv, so the check cannot start`;
  }

  spell(a: Assertion): string {
    return spellPython(a);
  }
}

===== packages/adapters/src/python/ini.ts =====
/**
 * The INI reading pytest does through iniconfig (2.x `_parse.py`), ported
 * line for line, and the two pattern languages pytest matches with:
 * `fnmatch` for names and paths, and `shlex.split` for an `args` setting.
 * Ported rather than run, since nothing on the host may start Python (I1).
 */
import { refuse } from '../refusal.js';

const COMMENT = '#;';

/** Sections by name, each key's value its lines joined by `\n`, as iniconfig yields them. */
export type Ini = ReadonlyMap<string, ReadonlyMap<string, string>>;

export function parseIni(text: string, path: string): Ini {
  const sections = new Map<string, Map<string, string[]>>();
  let section: string | null = null;
  let last: string[] | null = null;
  const lines = text.replace(/^\uFEFF/u, '').split(/\r\n|\n|\r/u);
  lines.forEach((raw, index) => {
    const at = `${path}:${String(index + 1)}`;
    const stripped = raw.trimStart();
    let line = stripped !== '' && COMMENT.includes(stripped[0] ?? '') ? '' : raw.trimEnd();
    if (line === '') return;
    if (line.startsWith('[')) {
      let head = line;
      for (const c of COMMENT) head = (head.split(c)[0] ?? '').trimEnd();
      if (head.endsWith(']')) {
        const name = head.slice(1, -1);
        if (name === '') refuse('unparseable', `${at}: empty section name`);
        if (sections.has(name)) refuse('unparseable', `${at}: duplicate section ${name}`);
        sections.set(name, new Map());
        section = name;
        last = null;
        return;
      }
      line = ` ${line.trim()}`; // iniconfig reads a bracket line that is no header as a continuation
    }
    if (!/^\s/u.test(line)) {
      let split = line.indexOf('=');
      if (split === -1 || line.slice(0, split).includes(':')) split = line.indexOf(':');
      if (split === -1) refuse('unparseable', `${at}: unexpected line`);
      if (section === null) refuse('unparseable', `${at}: no section header defined`);
      const name = line.slice(0, split).trim();
      const value = line.slice(split + 1).trim();
      const keys = sections.get(section);
      if (keys === undefined) return;
      if (keys.has(name)) refuse('unparseable', `${at}: duplicate name ${name}`);
      last = value === '' ? [] : [value];
      keys.set(name, last);
      return;
    }
    if (last === null) refuse('unparseable', `${at}: unexpected value continuation`);
    last.push(line.trim());
  });
  return new Map([...sections].map(([name, keys]) => [name, new Map([...keys].map(([k, v]) => [k, v.join('\n')]))]));
}

/**
 * `fnmatch.fnmatch` on a POSIX host, where it is case-sensitive: `*` matches
 * anything, `/` included; `?` one character; `[seq]` and `[!seq]` a class.
 */
export function fnmatch(name: string, pattern: string): boolean {
  let source = '';
  for (let i = 0; i < pattern.length; i++) {
    const c = pattern[i] ?? '';
    if (c === '*') source += '[\\s\\S]*';
    else if (c === '?') source += '[\\s\\S]';
    else if (c === '[') {
      const close = pattern.indexOf(']', i + 2);
      if (close === -1) source += '\\[';
      else {
        let body = pattern.slice(i + 1, close);
        const negated = body.startsWith('!');
        if (negated) body = body.slice(1);
        source += `[${negated ? '^' : ''}${body.replace(/[\\\]^]/gu, (m) => `\\${m}`)}]`;
        i = close;
      }
    } else source += c.replace(/[.*+?^${}()|[\]\\/]/gu, (m) => `\\${m}`);
  }
  return new RegExp(`^${source}$`, 'u').test(name);
}

/**
 * `shlex.split` for the values pytest reads as `args`, refused where the
 * value quotes or escapes, whose splitting the parse would have to model
 * exactly to name the same paths pytest does.
 */
export function splitArgs(value: string, label: string): string[] {
  if (/["'\\]/u.test(value)) refuse('unresolvable-config', `${label} quotes or escapes a value, which is not read statically`);
  return value.split(/\s+/u).filter((part) => part !== '');
}

===== packages/adapters/src/python/install.ts =====
/**
 * Python's dependency installs (D-A-PY-04, A-R29-02, D-R29-05). Each makes a
 * virtualenv with copies, since a linked interpreter points out of the tree
 * and R34 refuses the link, then installs into it what the lockfile pins,
 * every package verified against the lockfile's hash. Nothing is built: a
 * package with no wheel, and the project itself, would run their own code to
 * build, so neither is installed and a lockfile that needs either is
 * refused, naming it.
 */
import { parse as parseToml } from 'smol-toml';
import type { DependencyInstaller, LockedPackage } from '../types.js';
import { PYTHON_DEPENDENCY_TREES } from '../trees.js';
import { requirementLines, requirementName } from './config.js';

/** Python 3.13 with uv 0.9.13, pinned by digest: where a Python tree is installed and every check over it runs (D-A-PY-07). */
export const PYTHON_IMAGE = 'ghcr.io/astral-sh/uv@sha256:c5db46150ecdfa1dcb35f2c666cb027624ebaad1781d0d7740ccccbdb53b2460';

/** The hosts a Python install downloads from: the index and the files it links to. */
export const PYPI_REGISTRIES: readonly string[] = Object.freeze(['pypi.org', 'files.pythonhosted.org']);

/** The first step of both: a virtualenv whose interpreter is a copy, inside the tree. */
const VENV = Object.freeze(['python', '-m', 'venv', '--copies', '.venv'] as const);

function isRecord(value: unknown): value is Record<string, unknown> {
  return typeof value === 'object' && value !== null && !Array.isArray(value) && !(value instanceof Date);
}

const SHA256 = /^sha256:[0-9a-f]{64}$/u;

/**
 * `uv.lock`, version 1. The project and its workspace members are not
 * installed (`--no-install-workspace`); every other package must come from
 * an `https` registry with a wheel, each wheel hashed.
 */
function uvPackages(document: unknown): ReturnType<DependencyInstaller['packages']> {
  if (typeof document !== 'string') return { ok: false, reason: 'uv.lock was not read as text' };
  let lock: Record<string, unknown>;
  try {
    lock = parseToml(document);
  } catch (error) {
    return { ok: false, reason: `uv.lock is not TOML: ${error instanceof Error ? error.message : String(error)}` };
  }
  if (lock.version !== 1) return { ok: false, reason: `uv.lock is lockfile version ${JSON.stringify(lock.version)}; only version 1 is read` };
  const manifest = isRecord(lock.manifest) ? lock.manifest : {};
  const members = new Set(Array.isArray(manifest.members) ? manifest.members.filter((m): m is string => typeof m === 'string').map((m) => requirementName(m) ?? m) : []);
  if (!Array.isArray(lock.package)) return { ok: true, packages: [] };
  const packages: LockedPackage[] = [];
  for (const entry of lock.package) {
    if (!isRecord(entry) || typeof entry.name !== 'string' || typeof entry.version !== 'string' && entry.version !== undefined) return { ok: false, reason: 'uv.lock has a package with no name' };
    const key = `${entry.name}@${typeof entry.version === 'string' ? entry.version : '(no version)'}`;
    const source = isRecord(entry.source) ? entry.source : {};
    const local = source.virtual ?? source.editable;
    if (members.has(requirementName(entry.name) ?? entry.name) || local === '.') continue;
    let unverifiable: string | null = null;
    const wheels = Array.isArray(entry.wheels) ? entry.wheels : [];
    const hashes = wheels.map((w) => (isRecord(w) && typeof w.hash === 'string' ? w.hash : null));
    if (typeof source.registry !== 'string') unverifiable = `it comes from ${Object.keys(source).join(', ') || 'no source'}, not a registry`;
    else if (!source.registry.startsWith('https://')) unverifiable = `it comes from ${source.registry}, not an https registry`;
    else if (wheels.length === 0) unverifiable = 'it has no wheel, and building it from source runs its code';
    else if (hashes.some((h) => h === null || !SHA256.test(h))) unverifiable = 'a wheel of it records no sha256 hash';
    const integrity = hashes.every((h): h is string => h !== null) && hashes.length > 0 ? [...hashes].sort().join(' ') : null;
    packages.push({ key, integrity, unverifiable });
  }
  return { ok: true, packages: packages.sort((a, b) => a.key.localeCompare(b.key)) };
}

/**
 * A hashed `requirements.txt`: one `name==version` per line with a
 * `--hash=sha256:` for each file it may install. An option line, a URL, an
 * editable, or a nested file is a document this installer does not read,
 * since each names code from somewhere the hashes do not cover.
 */
function pipPackages(document: unknown): ReturnType<DependencyInstaller['packages']> {
  if (typeof document !== 'string') return { ok: false, reason: 'requirements.txt was not read as text' };
  const packages: LockedPackage[] = [];
  for (const { line, text } of requirementLines(document)) {
    if (text.startsWith('-')) return { ok: false, reason: `requirements.txt line ${String(line)} is the option ${text.split(/\s/u)[0] ?? text}, which names packages or an index outside the file's hashes` };
    const tokens = text.split(/\s+/u);
    const hashes = tokens.filter((t) => t.startsWith('--hash=')).map((t) => t.slice('--hash='.length));
    const other = tokens.filter((t) => t.startsWith('--') && !t.startsWith('--hash='));
    if (other.length > 0) return { ok: false, reason: `requirements.txt line ${String(line)} passes ${other.join(' ')}` };
    const spec = tokens.filter((t) => !t.startsWith('--')).join(' ');
    const name = requirementName(spec);
    if (name === undefined) return { ok: false, reason: `requirements.txt line ${String(line)} names no package` };
    const pin = /^[A-Za-z0-9._-]+(?:\[[^\]]*\])?\s*==\s*([0-9][0-9A-Za-z.+!-]*)\s*(?:;.*)?$/u.exec(spec);
    let unverifiable: string | null = null;
    if (/@|:\/\//u.test(spec.split(';')[0] ?? '')) unverifiable = 'it is a direct reference, not a registry download';
    else if (pin?.[1] === undefined) unverifiable = 'it is not pinned to one version with ==';
    else if (hashes.length === 0) unverifiable = 'it records no hash';
    else if (hashes.some((h) => !SHA256.test(h))) unverifiable = 'a hash of it is not sha256';
    packages.push({ key: `${name}@${pin?.[1] ?? '(unpinned)'}`, integrity: hashes.length === 0 ? null : [...hashes].sort().join(' '), unverifiable });
  }
  return { ok: true, packages: packages.sort((a, b) => a.key.localeCompare(b.key)) };
}

const HASH_FAILED = /Hash mismatch for|DO NOT MATCH THE HASHES/u;
const NO_WHEEL = /Building source distributions is disabled|can't be installed because it doesn't have a source distribution or wheel|from versions: none/u;
const UNREACHABLE = /Failed to fetch|error sending request|Could not connect|ConnectError|ProxyError|NewConnectionError|Temporary failure in name resolution|Name or service not known|\b40[37]\b/u;
const NO_INTERPRETER = /No interpreter found|requires-python|is incompatible with the project's Python requirement/u;

function failure(output: string): string | null {
  if (HASH_FAILED.test(output)) return 'a package failed the hash its lockfile records';
  if (NO_WHEEL.test(output)) return 'a package has no wheel for the install image, and building one from source is refused';
  if (NO_INTERPRETER.test(output)) return "the install image's Python does not satisfy the project's requires-python";
  if (UNREACHABLE.test(output)) return 'a granted registry could not be reached';
  return null;
}

/** `uv.lock`: a copied virtualenv, then uv installs exactly the lockfile into it, building nothing and installing no project. */
export const UV_INSTALLER: DependencyInstaller = Object.freeze({
  stack: 'uv',
  lockfile: 'uv.lock',
  format: 'text',
  manifest: 'pyproject.toml',
  files: Object.freeze(['pyproject.toml', 'uv.lock', '.python-version']),
  nested: 'pyproject.toml',
  trees: PYTHON_DEPENDENCY_TREES,
  image: PYTHON_IMAGE,
  steps: Object.freeze([
    VENV,
    Object.freeze(['uv', 'sync', '--frozen', '--no-install-workspace', '--no-build', '--all-extras', '--all-groups'] as const),
  ] as const),
  env: Object.freeze({
    HOME: '/tmp', UV_CACHE_DIR: '/tmp/uv-cache', UV_NO_CONFIG: '1', UV_PYTHON_DOWNLOADS: 'never', UV_LINK_MODE: 'copy', UV_NO_PROGRESS: '1',
  }),
  packages: uvPackages,
  failure,
});

/** A hashed `requirements.txt`: a copied virtualenv, then pip installs each pinned wheel by hash, and nothing it depends on unlisted. */
export const PIP_INSTALLER: DependencyInstaller = Object.freeze({
  stack: 'pip',
  lockfile: 'requirements.txt',
  format: 'text',
  manifest: 'pyproject.toml',
  files: Object.freeze(['requirements.txt', '.python-version']),
  nested: null,
  trees: PYTHON_DEPENDENCY_TREES,
  image: PYTHON_IMAGE,
  steps: Object.freeze([
    VENV,
    Object.freeze([
      '.venv/bin/python', '-m', 'pip', 'install', '--require-hashes', '--no-deps', '--only-binary', ':all:', '--no-input',
      '--disable-pip-version-check', '--no-cache-dir', '-r', 'requirements.txt',
    ] as const),
  ] as const),
  env: Object.freeze({ HOME: '/tmp', PIP_CONFIG_FILE: '/dev/null', PIP_NO_INPUT: '1', PIP_DISABLE_PIP_VERSION_CHECK: '1' }),
  packages: pipPackages,
  failure,
});

===== packages/adapters/src/python/parse.ts =====
/**
 * Python source, parsed on the host by a grammar written in TypeScript, so
 * reading a test file runs nothing (I1). A file the grammar cannot read
 * whole is refused, never read in part: a part would hold fewer assertions
 * and cases than the file, which reads as a deletion (D-R29-12).
 */
import type { SyntaxNode, Tree } from '@lezer/common';
import { parser } from '@lezer/python';
import { readRegularFile, SOURCE_FILE_CAP } from '../files.js';
import { refuse } from '../refusal.js';

export interface PythonModule {
  readonly file: string;
  readonly text: string;
  readonly tree: Tree;
  /** The offset each line starts at, 1-based by index + 1. */
  readonly lineStarts: readonly number[];
}

export function parsePython(text: string, file: string): PythonModule {
  const tree = parser.parse(text);
  const lineStarts = [0];
  for (let i = 0; i < text.length; i++) if (text[i] === '\n') lineStarts.push(i + 1);
  const module: PythonModule = { file, text, tree, lineStarts };
  let error: number | undefined;
  tree.iterate({
    enter: (node) => {
      if (error !== undefined) return false;
      if (node.type.isError) error = node.from;
      return undefined;
    },
  });
  if (error !== undefined) refuse('unparseable', `${file} does not parse at line ${String(lineOf(module, error))}`);
  return module;
}

export async function readPython(file: string): Promise<PythonModule> {
  return parsePython(await readRegularFile(file, SOURCE_FILE_CAP), file);
}

/** The 1-based line of an offset. */
export function lineOf(module: PythonModule, pos: number): number {
  let lo = 0;
  let hi = module.lineStarts.length - 1;
  while (lo < hi) {
    const mid = (lo + hi + 1) >> 1;
    if ((module.lineStarts[mid] ?? 0) <= pos) lo = mid;
    else hi = mid - 1;
  }
  return lo + 1;
}

export function textOf(module: PythonModule, node: SyntaxNode): string {
  return module.text.slice(node.from, node.to);
}

/** Source text with its whitespace collapsed: the form an assertion's argument is compared in. */
export function flatText(module: PythonModule, node: SyntaxNode): string {
  return textOf(module, node).replace(/\s+/gu, ' ').trim();
}

/** Every direct child, in order. */
export function childrenOf(node: SyntaxNode): SyntaxNode[] {
  const out: SyntaxNode[] = [];
  for (let child = node.firstChild; child !== null; child = child.nextSibling) out.push(child);
  return out;
}

const PUNCTUATION = new Set(['(', ')', '[', ']', '{', '}', ',', ':', '.', '=', 'AssignOp', 'At']);

/** The children that carry meaning: no brackets, commas, or keywords spelled as their own node. */
export function partsOf(node: SyntaxNode): SyntaxNode[] {
  return childrenOf(node).filter((c) => !PUNCTUATION.has(c.name) && !/^[a-z]+$/u.test(c.name));
}

/** The statements of a `Script` or a `Body`. */
export function statementsOf(node: SyntaxNode): SyntaxNode[] {
  return childrenOf(node).filter((c) => /Statement$|Definition$/u.test(c.name));
}

/**
 * A dotted name as written: `pytest.mark.skip` from a `MemberExpression`
 * chain or a `Decorator`'s flat run of names, or undefined for anything
 * that is not one.
 */
export function dottedName(module: PythonModule, node: SyntaxNode): string | undefined {
  if (node.name === 'VariableName') return textOf(module, node);
  if (node.name === 'MemberExpression') {
    const parts = childrenOf(node);
    const head = parts[0];
    const tail = parts[parts.length - 1];
    if (head === undefined || tail?.name !== 'PropertyName') return undefined;
    const left = dottedName(module, head);
    return left === undefined ? undefined : `${left}.${textOf(module, tail)}`;
  }
  return undefined;
}

/** A decorator's dotted name and its argument list, if it is called. */
export function decoratorOf(module: PythonModule, decorator: SyntaxNode): { readonly name: string; readonly args: SyntaxNode | null } | undefined {
  const names: string[] = [];
  let args: SyntaxNode | null = null;
  for (const child of childrenOf(decorator)) {
    if (child.name === 'At' || child.name === '.') continue;
    if (child.name === 'VariableName' || child.name === 'PropertyName') {
      if (args !== null) return undefined;
      names.push(textOf(module, child));
    } else if (child.name === 'ArgList' && args === null) args = child;
    else return undefined;
  }
  return names.length === 0 ? undefined : { name: names.join('.'), args };
}

/** A call's callee name and its argument list, when the callee is a dotted name. */
export function callOf(module: PythonModule, node: SyntaxNode): { readonly name: string; readonly args: SyntaxNode } | undefined {
  if (node.name !== 'CallExpression') return undefined;
  const [callee, args] = childrenOf(node);
  if (callee === undefined || args?.name !== 'ArgList') return undefined;
  const name = dottedName(module, callee);
  return name === undefined ? undefined : { name, args };
}

/** An argument list's positional arguments and keyword arguments, or undefined when it spreads (`*a`, `**k`). */
export function argumentsOf(module: PythonModule, args: SyntaxNode): { readonly positional: SyntaxNode[]; readonly keyword: Map<string, SyntaxNode> } | undefined {
  const positional: SyntaxNode[] = [];
  const keyword = new Map<string, SyntaxNode>();
  const children = childrenOf(args).filter((c) => c.name !== '(' && c.name !== ')' && c.name !== ',');
  for (let i = 0; i < children.length; i++) {
    const child = children[i];
    if (child === undefined) continue;
    if (child.name === '*' || child.name === '**') return undefined;
    const next = children[i + 1];
    if (child.name === 'VariableName' && next?.name === 'AssignOp') {
      const value = children[i + 2];
      if (value === undefined) return undefined;
      keyword.set(textOf(module, child), value);
      i += 2;
      continue;
    }
    if (keyword.size > 0) return undefined;
    positional.push(child);
  }
  return { positional, keyword };
}

/**
 * A Python string literal's value, when it is a plain one: quoted, with no
 * prefix and no escape. Anything else (an f-string, bytes, a raw string, an
 * escape, implicit concatenation) is undefined, for the caller to treat as
 * a value it cannot see.
 */
export function stringValue(module: PythonModule, node: SyntaxNode): string | undefined {
  if (node.name !== 'String') return undefined;
  const raw = textOf(module, node);
  const match = /^("""|'''|"|')([\s\S]*)\1$/u.exec(raw);
  if (match === null) return undefined;
  const body = match[2] ?? '';
  if (body.includes('\\')) return undefined;
  return body;
}

===== packages/adapters/src/python/tests.ts =====
/**
 * What a pytest file asserts, skips, and declares, read from its parse with
 * nothing run (I1). Assertions are `assert` statements, `self.assert*`
 * calls, `pytest.raises` and `pytest.warns`, and `pytest.approx` with its
 * tolerance. Skip markers are pytest's marks and calls and unittest's
 * decorators and `skipTest`. Cases are the functions and methods pytest
 * collects, by the configured name patterns, with each parametrised row's
 * id where every value in it is a literal (D-R29-07).
 */
import type { SyntaxNode } from '@lezer/common';
import { pairAssertions, type AssertionRules } from '../assertions.js';
import type { Assertion, AssertionDelta } from '../types.js';
import type { PytestRules } from './config.js';
import { fnmatch } from './ini.js';
import {
  argumentsOf, callOf, childrenOf, decoratorOf, flatText, lineOf, partsOf, statementsOf, stringValue, textOf, type PythonModule,
} from './parse.js';

// ---- assertions ------------------------------------------------------------

const COMPARE_KEYWORDS = new Set(['is', 'not', 'in']);

/** The operator of a comparison `BinaryExpression`, `==` or `is not` or `not in`, or undefined for any other binary. */
function comparisonOf(module: PythonModule, node: SyntaxNode): string | undefined {
  const ops = childrenOf(node).slice(1, -1);
  if (ops.length === 0) return undefined;
  if (ops.length === 1 && ops[0]?.name === 'CompareOp') return textOf(module, ops[0]);
  if (ops.every((o) => COMPARE_KEYWORDS.has(o.name))) return ops.map((o) => textOf(module, o)).join(' ');
  return undefined;
}

/** A chain `a < b <= c` as its operands and operators, read through the left-nested parse. */
function chainOf(module: PythonModule, node: SyntaxNode): { operators: string[]; operands: SyntaxNode[] } | undefined {
  const operator = comparisonOf(module, node);
  if (node.name !== 'BinaryExpression' || operator === undefined) return undefined;
  const parts = childrenOf(node);
  const left = parts[0];
  const right = parts[parts.length - 1];
  if (left === undefined || right === undefined) return undefined;
  const inner = chainOf(module, left);
  if (inner === undefined) return { operators: [operator], operands: [left, right] };
  return { operators: [...inner.operators, operator], operands: [...inner.operands, right] };
}

/** A numeric literal's value, signed, or undefined. */
function numberOf(module: PythonModule, node: SyntaxNode | undefined): number | undefined {
  if (node === undefined) return undefined;
  if (node.name === 'Number') {
    const text = textOf(module, node).replaceAll('_', '');
    return /^(?:\d+\.?\d*|\.\d+)(?:[eE][+-]?\d+)?$/u.test(text) ? Number(text) : undefined;
  }
  if (node.name === 'UnaryExpression') {
    const [sign, operand] = childrenOf(node);
    const value = numberOf(module, operand);
    if (value === undefined || sign === undefined) return undefined;
    const s = textOf(module, sign);
    return s === '-' ? -value : s === '+' ? value : undefined;
  }
  return undefined;
}

const APPROX = new Set(['pytest.approx', 'approx']);

/**
 * `x == pytest.approx(v, rel=r, abs=a)`. pytest's default band is `rel=1e-6`;
 * with only `abs` given, `rel` is not applied at all. A tolerance is one
 * comparable number only where one bound is given or none: with both, the
 * pair is in the arguments and any change to it is reported (D-R29-07).
 */
function approxAssertion(module: PythonModule, operator: string, subject: SyntaxNode, call: { name: string; args: SyntaxNode }, line: number, file: string): Assertion | undefined {
  if (!APPROX.has(call.name)) return undefined;
  const args = argumentsOf(module, call.args);
  const expected = args?.positional[0];
  if (args === undefined || expected === undefined) return undefined;
  const rel = args.keyword.get('rel') ?? args.positional[1];
  const abs = args.keyword.get('abs') ?? args.positional[2];
  const base = [flatText(module, subject), flatText(module, expected)];
  const relValue = numberOf(module, rel);
  const absValue = numberOf(module, abs);
  if (rel === undefined && abs === undefined) return { file, line, operator: `${operator} approx`, args: base, tolerance: 1e-6 };
  if (abs === undefined && relValue !== undefined) return { file, line, operator: `${operator} approx`, args: base, tolerance: relValue };
  if (rel === undefined && absValue !== undefined) return { file, line, operator: `${operator} approx abs`, args: base, tolerance: absValue };
  const bounds = [rel === undefined ? null : `rel=${flatText(module, rel)}`, abs === undefined ? null : `abs=${flatText(module, abs)}`].filter((b): b is string => b !== null);
  return { file, line, operator: `${operator} approx bounds`, args: [...base, ...bounds] };
}

function assertStatement(module: PythonModule, node: SyntaxNode, file: string): Assertion {
  const line = lineOf(module, node.from);
  const [test] = partsOf(node);
  if (test === undefined) return { file, line, operator: 'truthy', args: [''] };
  const chain = chainOf(module, test);
  if (chain !== undefined) {
    const [subject, expected] = chain.operands;
    const call = expected === undefined ? undefined : callOf(module, expected);
    if (chain.operators.length === 1 && subject !== undefined && call !== undefined && (chain.operators[0] === '==' || chain.operators[0] === '!=')) {
      const approx = approxAssertion(module, chain.operators[0], subject, call, line, file);
      if (approx !== undefined) return approx;
    }
    return { file, line, operator: chain.operators.join(','), args: chain.operands.map((o) => flatText(module, o)) };
  }
  if (test.name === 'UnaryExpression' && textOf(module, test).startsWith('not')) {
    const operand = childrenOf(test)[1];
    return { file, line, operator: 'not', args: [operand === undefined ? '' : flatText(module, operand)] };
  }
  return { file, line, operator: 'truthy', args: [flatText(module, test)] };
}

/** unittest's almost-equal: `places` (default 7) as the band it implies, 0.5·10^-places, or `delta` as given. */
function almostEqual(module: PythonModule, method: string, args: NonNullable<ReturnType<typeof argumentsOf>>, line: number, file: string): Assertion {
  const operands = args.positional.slice(0, 2).map((p) => flatText(module, p));
  const delta = args.keyword.get('delta');
  if (delta !== undefined) {
    const value = numberOf(module, delta);
    return value === undefined ? { file, line, operator: `${method} delta`, args: [...operands, `delta=${flatText(module, delta)}`] } : { file, line, operator: `${method} delta`, args: operands, tolerance: value };
  }
  const placesNode = args.keyword.get('places') ?? args.positional[2];
  if (placesNode === undefined) return { file, line, operator: method, args: operands, tolerance: 0.5 * 10 ** -7 };
  const places = numberOf(module, placesNode);
  if (places === undefined) return { file, line, operator: method, args: [...operands, `places=${flatText(module, placesNode)}`] };
  return { file, line, operator: method, args: operands, tolerance: 0.5 * 10 ** -places };
}

const RAISES = new Set(['pytest.raises', 'raises', 'pytest.warns', 'warns', 'pytest.deprecated_call']);

function callAssertion(module: PythonModule, node: SyntaxNode, file: string): Assertion | undefined {
  const call = callOf(module, node);
  if (call === undefined) return undefined;
  const line = lineOf(module, node.from);
  const args = argumentsOf(module, call.args);
  const method = /^(?:self|cls)\.(assert\w*|fail\w*)$/u.exec(call.name)?.[1];
  if (method !== undefined) {
    if (args === undefined) return { file, line, operator: method, args: [flatText(module, call.args)] };
    if (method === 'assertAlmostEqual' || method === 'assertNotAlmostEqual') return almostEqual(module, method, args, line, file);
    return { file, line, operator: method, args: args.positional.map((p) => flatText(module, p)) };
  }
  if (RAISES.has(call.name)) {
    const kinds = call.name.endsWith('raises') ? 'raises' : call.name.endsWith('warns') ? 'warns' : 'deprecated_call';
    if (args === undefined) return { file, line, operator: kinds, args: [flatText(module, call.args)] };
    const match = args.keyword.get('match');
    return { file, line, operator: kinds, args: [...args.positional.map((p) => flatText(module, p)), ...(match === undefined ? [] : [`match=${flatText(module, match)}`])] };
  }
  return undefined;
}

export function extractAssertions(module: PythonModule): Assertion[] {
  const out: Assertion[] = [];
  module.tree.iterate({
    enter: (ref) => {
      const node = ref.node;
      if (node.name === 'AssertStatement') out.push(assertStatement(module, node, module.file));
      else if (node.name === 'CallExpression') {
        const found = callAssertion(module, node, module.file);
        if (found !== undefined) out.push(found);
      }
    },
  });
  return out;
}

function isNegated(operator: string): boolean {
  return /^(?:!=|not\b|is not|not in|assertNot|assertIsNot|assertFalse)/u.test(operator) || operator.includes(',not') || operator.startsWith('!=');
}

/** A literal the parse can judge truthy: a non-zero number, a non-empty string, `True`. */
function truthyLiteral(text: string | undefined): boolean {
  if (text === undefined) return false;
  if (text === 'True') return true;
  if (/^-?\d+(?:\.\d+)?$/u.test(text)) return Number(text) !== 0;
  return /^(["']).+\1$/u.test(text);
}

/**
 * Whether `after` provably checks at least what `before` did. Only the
 * strengthenings below are recognised; any other change on the subject is
 * reported, the direction that fails closed.
 */
function atLeastAsStrong(before: Assertion, after: Assertion): boolean {
  if (isNegated(before.operator) || isNegated(after.operator)) return false;
  const value = after.args[1];
  if (before.operator === after.operator && after.args.length > before.args.length && before.args.every((a, i) => after.args[i] === a)) return true;
  const equality = after.operator === '==' || after.operator === 'assertEqual';
  if ((before.operator === 'truthy' || before.operator === 'assertTrue') && equality) return truthyLiteral(value);
  if ((before.operator === 'is not' || before.operator === 'assertIsNotNone') && equality) {
    return value !== undefined && value !== 'None' && (before.operator === 'assertIsNotNone' || before.args[1] === 'None');
  }
  return false;
}

export const PYTHON_ASSERTION_RULES: AssertionRules = { atLeastAsStrong, negated: isNegated };

export function comparePythonAssertions(before: readonly Assertion[], after: readonly Assertion[]): AssertionDelta {
  return pairAssertions(before, after, PYTHON_ASSERTION_RULES);
}

/** `line 3: assert a == b`, or `line 4: self.assertEqual(a, b)`. */
export function spellPython(a: Assertion): string {
  const at = `line ${String(a.line)}: `;
  const tolerance = a.tolerance === undefined ? '' : ` (tolerance ${String(a.tolerance)})`;
  if (/^assert|^fail/u.test(a.operator) || ['raises', 'warns', 'deprecated_call'].includes(a.operator)) return `${at}${a.operator}(${a.args.join(', ')})${tolerance}`;
  if (a.operator === 'truthy') return `${at}assert ${a.args[0] ?? ''}`;
  if (a.operator === 'not') return `${at}assert not ${a.args[0] ?? ''}`;
  const ops = a.operator.split(',');
  let text = a.args[0] ?? '';
  ops.forEach((op, i) => { text += ` ${op} ${a.args[i + 1] ?? ''}`; });
  return `${at}assert ${text}${a.args.length > ops.length + 1 ? ` [${a.args.slice(ops.length + 1).join(', ')}]` : ''}${tolerance}`;
}

// ---- names, marks, and ids --------------------------------------------------

/** `_matches_prefix_or_glob_option`: a prefix, or a glob where the option holds a wildcard. */
export function matchesOption(name: string, options: readonly string[]): boolean {
  return options.some((option) => name.startsWith(option) || (/[*?[]/u.test(option) && fnmatch(name, option)));
}

interface Decorated {
  readonly node: SyntaxNode;
  readonly decorators: ReadonlyArray<{ readonly name: string; readonly args: SyntaxNode | null; readonly line: number }>;
}

/** A definition with its decorators, nearest the `def` first, as pytest stores the marks they apply. */
function decorated(module: PythonModule, statement: SyntaxNode): Decorated | undefined {
  if (statement.name === 'FunctionDefinition' || statement.name === 'ClassDefinition') return { node: statement, decorators: [] };
  if (statement.name !== 'DecoratedStatement') return undefined;
  const children = childrenOf(statement);
  const node = children.find((c) => c.name === 'FunctionDefinition' || c.name === 'ClassDefinition');
  if (node === undefined) return undefined;
  const decorators = children.filter((c) => c.name === 'Decorator').map((d) => {
    const read = decoratorOf(module, d);
    return { name: read?.name ?? flatText(module, d), args: read?.args ?? null, line: lineOf(module, d.from) };
  });
  return { node, decorators: decorators.reverse() };
}

function nameOf(module: PythonModule, def: SyntaxNode): string {
  const name = childrenOf(def).find((c) => c.name === 'VariableName');
  return name === undefined ? '' : textOf(module, name);
}
