const SKIP_DECORATORS = /(?:^|\.)(?:mark\.(?:skip|skipif|xfail)|skip|skipIf|skipUnless|expectedFailure)$/u;
const SKIP_CALLS = /^(?:pytest\.(?:skip|xfail|importorskip)|self\.skipTest|cls\.skipTest|skip|importorskip)$/u;
const PARAMETRIZE = /^(?:pytest\.)?mark\.parametrize$/u;
const FIXTURE = /(?:^|\.)fixture$/u;
const UNITTEST_BASES = new Set(['TestCase', 'unittest.TestCase', 'IsolatedAsyncioTestCase', 'unittest.IsolatedAsyncioTestCase']);
const PLAIN_BASES = new Set(['object']);

/** The id pytest makes for one value (`IdMaker._idval`), or undefined where it depends on what the parse cannot see. */
function idOf(module: PythonModule, value: SyntaxNode, argname: string, index: number, rules: PytestRules): string | undefined {
  const str = stringValue(module, value);
  if (str !== undefined) {
    // `ascii_escaped` leaves printable ASCII alone; anything else it rewrites, which is not modelled.
    if (!/^[\x20-\x7e]*$/u.test(str)) return undefined;
    return str.length > 100 && rules.longStringIds === 'short' ? `${argname}${String(index)}` : str;
  }
  if (value.name === 'Boolean' || value.name === 'None') return textOf(module, value);
  const number = numberOf(module, value);
  if (number !== undefined) {
    const text = textOf(module, value).replaceAll('_', '').replace(/^\+/u, '');
    if (/^-?\s*\d+$/u.test(text)) return String(BigInt(text.replace(/\s/gu, '')));
    // Python prints a float in fixed notation from 1e-4 to 1e16, as JavaScript does over that range, with `.0` when whole.
    const magnitude = Math.abs(number);
    if (magnitude === 0 || (magnitude >= 1e-4 && magnitude < 1e16)) return Number.isInteger(number) ? `${String(number)}.0` : String(number);
    return undefined;
  }
  if (['ArrayExpression', 'TupleExpression', 'DictionaryExpression', 'SetExpression', 'ArrayComprehensionExpression'].includes(value.name)) return `${argname}${String(index)}`;
  return undefined;
}

/** `make_unique_parameterset_ids`: a repeated id takes a counter, after `_` where it ends in a digit. */
function unique(ids: readonly string[]): string[] {
  const counts = new Map<string, number>();
  for (const id of ids) counts.set(id, (counts.get(id) ?? 0) + 1);
  const resolved = [...ids];
  const suffixes = new Map<string, number>();
  resolved.forEach((id, index) => {
    if ((counts.get(id) ?? 0) <= 1) return;
    const sep = /\d$/u.test(id) ? '_' : '';
    let n = suffixes.get(id) ?? 0;
    let candidate = `${id}${sep}${String(n)}`;
    while (resolved.includes(candidate)) candidate = `${id}${sep}${String(++n)}`;
    resolved[index] = candidate;
    suffixes.set(id, n + 1);
  });
  return resolved;
}

/** One `parametrize` mark's ids, and the skip marks its `pytest.param` rows carry, or undefined where a row is not literal. */
function parametrizeIds(module: PythonModule, args: SyntaxNode | null, rules: PytestRules): { ids: string[]; skips: Array<{ id: string; mark: string }> } | undefined {
  if (args === null) return undefined;
  const read = argumentsOf(module, args);
  const [namesNode, valuesNode] = read?.positional ?? [];
  if (read === undefined || namesNode === undefined) return undefined;
  const values = valuesNode ?? read.keyword.get('argvalues');
  if (values === undefined || (values.name !== 'ArrayExpression' && values.name !== 'TupleExpression')) return undefined;
  const namesText = stringValue(module, namesNode);
  const argnames = namesText !== undefined
    ? namesText.split(',').map((n) => n.trim()).filter((n) => n !== '')
    : (namesNode.name === 'ArrayExpression' || namesNode.name === 'TupleExpression') ? partsOf(namesNode).map((n) => stringValue(module, n)) : [undefined];
  if (argnames.length === 0 || argnames.some((n) => n === undefined)) return undefined;
  const names = argnames as string[];
  const explicit = read.keyword.get('ids');
  let given: Array<string | null> | undefined;
  if (explicit !== undefined) {
    if (explicit.name !== 'ArrayExpression' && explicit.name !== 'TupleExpression') return undefined;
    given = [];
    for (const item of partsOf(explicit)) {
      if (item.name === 'None') given.push(null);
      else {
        const s = stringValue(module, item);
        if (s === undefined || !/^[\x20-\x7e]*$/u.test(s)) return undefined;
        given.push(s);
      }
    }
  }
  const rows = partsOf(values);
  if (rows.length === 0) return undefined;
  const ids: string[] = [];
  const skips: Array<{ index: number; mark: string }> = [];
  for (const [index, row] of rows.entries()) {
    let cells: SyntaxNode[];
    let rowId: string | undefined;
    const param = callOf(module, row);
    if (param !== undefined && (param.name === 'pytest.param' || param.name === 'param')) {
      const p = argumentsOf(module, param.args);
      if (p === undefined) return undefined;
      cells = p.positional;
      const id = p.keyword.get('id');
      if (id !== undefined) {
        rowId = stringValue(module, id);
        if (rowId === undefined || !/^[\x20-\x7e]*$/u.test(rowId)) return undefined;
      }
      const marks = p.keyword.get('marks');
      if (marks !== undefined) for (const m of /mark\.(?:skip|skipif|xfail)/u.exec(flatText(module, marks)) ?? []) skips.push({ index, mark: `pytest.param(marks=${m})` });
    } else if (names.length === 1) cells = [row];
    else if (row.name === 'TupleExpression' || row.name === 'ArrayExpression') cells = partsOf(row);
    else return undefined;
    if (cells.length !== names.length) return undefined;
    if (rowId !== undefined) ids.push(rowId);
    else if (given !== undefined && index < given.length && given[index] !== null) ids.push(given[index] ?? '');
    else {
      const parts = cells.map((cell, i) => idOf(module, cell, names[i] ?? '', index, rules));
      if (parts.some((p) => p === undefined)) return undefined;
      ids.push(parts.join('-'));
    }
  }
  const resolved = unique(ids);
  return { ids: resolved, skips: skips.map((s) => ({ id: resolved[s.index] ?? '', mark: s.mark })) };
}

// ---- the walk ----------------------------------------------------------------

export interface PythonTestFile {
  /** The cases pytest collects, `Class > method[id]`. */
  readonly cases: string[];
  /** Each skip mark or call, `mark: case`. */
  readonly skips: string[];
}

/** A class's own body statements, and its bases as written. */
function classParts(module: PythonModule, cls: SyntaxNode): { body: SyntaxNode[]; bases: string[] } {
  const args = childrenOf(cls).find((c) => c.name === 'ArgList');
  const bases = args === undefined ? [] : partsOf(args).map((b) => flatText(module, b));
  const body = childrenOf(cls).find((c) => c.name === 'Body');
  return { body: body === undefined ? [] : statementsOf(body), bases };
}

/** Whether a statement assigns `name = value` exactly. */
function assigns(module: PythonModule, statement: SyntaxNode, name: string): SyntaxNode | undefined {
  if (statement.name !== 'AssignStatement') return undefined;
  const [target, op, value] = childrenOf(statement);
  return target?.name === 'VariableName' && textOf(module, target) === name && op?.name === 'AssignOp' ? value : undefined;
}

function skipCallsIn(module: PythonModule, node: SyntaxNode, owner: string, out: string[]): void {
  module.tree.iterate({
    from: node.from,
    to: node.to,
    enter: (ref) => {
      if (ref.name !== 'CallExpression') return;
      const call = callOf(module, ref.node);
      if (call !== undefined && SKIP_CALLS.test(call.name)) out.push(`${call.name}(): ${owner}`);
    },
  });
}

function marksOf(module: PythonModule, value: SyntaxNode | undefined): string[] {
  if (value === undefined) return [];
  return [...flatText(module, value).matchAll(/(?:pytest\.)?mark\.(?:skip|skipif|xfail)\b/gu)].map((m) => m[0]);
}

/**
 * The cases and skip markers of one file, under `rules`. A class is a test
 * class when its name matches `python_classes` or it derives from
 * unittest's `TestCase`, and it is passed over when it defines `__init__`
 * or `__new__` or sets `__test__ = False`. A base class the parse cannot see
 * contributes a case of its own, `Class > *Base`, so a change to what a
 * class inherits is a change to its cases (D-R29-07).
 */
export function readTestFile(module: PythonModule, rules: PytestRules): PythonTestFile {
  const cases: string[] = [];
  const skips: string[] = [];
  const top = statementsOf(module.tree.topNode);
  const classes = new Map<string, SyntaxNode>();
  for (const s of top) {
    const d = decorated(module, s);
    if (d?.node.name === 'ClassDefinition') classes.set(nameOf(module, d.node), d.node);
  }

  for (const s of top) {
    for (const mark of marksOf(module, assigns(module, s, 'pytestmark'))) skips.push(`pytestmark ${mark}: ${module.file}`);
    if (s.name === 'ExpressionStatement') {
      const call = callOf(module, childrenOf(s)[0] ?? s);
      if (call !== undefined && SKIP_CALLS.test(call.name)) skips.push(`${call.name}(): ${module.file}`);
    }
  }

  const isUnittest = (bases: readonly string[], seen: Set<string>): boolean => bases.some((b) => {
    if (UNITTEST_BASES.has(b)) return true;
    const local = classes.get(b);
    if (local === undefined || seen.has(b)) return false;
    seen.add(b);
    return isUnittest(classParts(module, local).bases, seen);
  });

  const functionCases = (d: Decorated, owner: string, inherited: Decorated['decorators'], unittest: boolean): void => {
    const name = nameOf(module, d.node);
    const decorators = [...d.decorators, ...inherited];
    if (decorators.some((x) => FIXTURE.test(x.name))) return;
    if (unittest ? !name.startsWith('test') : !matchesOption(name, rules.pythonFunctions)) return;
    const qualified = owner === '' ? name : `${owner} > ${name}`;
    for (const x of decorators) if (SKIP_DECORATORS.test(x.name)) skips.push(`${x.name}: ${qualified}`);
    skipCallsIn(module, d.node, qualified, skips);
    let ids: string[] | undefined = [''];
    if (!unittest) {
      for (const x of decorators.filter((y) => PARAMETRIZE.test(y.name))) {
        const read = parametrizeIds(module, x.args, rules);
        if (read === undefined || ids === undefined) {
          ids = undefined;
          continue;
        }
        for (const s of read.skips) skips.push(`${s.mark}: ${qualified}[${s.id}]`);
        ids = ids.flatMap((prefix) => read.ids.map((id) => (prefix === '' ? id : `${prefix}-${id}`)));
      }
    }
    if (ids === undefined || (ids.length === 1 && ids[0] === '')) cases.push(qualified);
    else for (const id of ids) cases.push(`${qualified}[${id}]`);
  };

  const classCases = (d: Decorated, owner: string, seen: Set<string>): void => {
    const name = nameOf(module, d.node);
    const { body, bases } = classParts(module, d.node);
    const unittest = isUnittest(bases, new Set());
    if (!unittest && !matchesOption(name, rules.pythonClasses)) return;
    const qualified = owner === '' ? name : `${owner} > ${name}`;
    const members = (statements: readonly SyntaxNode[]): Decorated[] => statements.map((s) => decorated(module, s)).filter((x): x is Decorated => x !== undefined);
    const own = members(body);
    if (own.some((m) => m.node.name === 'FunctionDefinition' && ['__init__', '__new__'].includes(nameOf(module, m.node)))) return;
    if (body.some((s) => assigns(module, s, '__test__') !== undefined && textOf(module, assigns(module, s, '__test__') ?? s) === 'False')) return;
    for (const x of d.decorators) if (SKIP_DECORATORS.test(x.name)) skips.push(`${x.name}: ${qualified}`);
    for (const s of body) for (const mark of marksOf(module, assigns(module, s, 'pytestmark'))) skips.push(`pytestmark ${mark}: ${qualified}`);
    const classMarks = [...d.decorators];
    const methods = new Map<string, Decorated>();
    const inherit = (bs: readonly string[], chain: Set<string>): void => {
      for (const base of bs) {
        if (UNITTEST_BASES.has(base) || PLAIN_BASES.has(base)) continue;
        const local = classes.get(base);
        if (local === undefined || chain.has(base)) {
          cases.push(`${qualified} > *${base}`);
          continue;
        }
        chain.add(base);
        const parts = classParts(module, local);
        inherit(parts.bases, chain);
        for (const m of members(parts.body)) if (m.node.name === 'FunctionDefinition') methods.set(nameOf(module, m.node), m);
      }
    };
    inherit(bases, new Set([name]));
    for (const m of own) if (m.node.name === 'FunctionDefinition') methods.set(nameOf(module, m.node), m);
    for (const m of methods.values()) functionCases(m, qualified, classMarks, unittest);
    for (const m of own) if (m.node.name === 'ClassDefinition' && !seen.has(nameOf(module, m.node))) classCases(m, qualified, new Set([...seen, nameOf(module, m.node)]));
  };

  for (const s of top) {
    const d = decorated(module, s);
    if (d === undefined) continue;
    if (d.node.name === 'FunctionDefinition') functionCases(d, '', [], false);
    else classCases(d, '', new Set([nameOf(module, d.node)]));
  }
  return { cases, skips };
}

===== packages/adapters/src/trees.ts =====
/**
 * The dependency trees each supported stack's install writes, by directory
 * name at any depth (A-R29-01, D-R29-10). A task's write under one is never
 * carried into the tree a check runs over, whatever the run's stack: a
 * `.venv` the change wrote would be its own judge, and a `__pycache__` file
 * is bytecode Python loads in place of the source its header names.
 */
export const NODE_DEPENDENCY_TREES: readonly string[] = Object.freeze(['node_modules']);
export const PYTHON_DEPENDENCY_TREES: readonly string[] = Object.freeze(['.venv', 'site-packages', '__pycache__']);

/** Every supported set's trees: what the carry rule, the tamper walk, and the adapters skip. */
export const DEPENDENCY_TREES: readonly string[] = Object.freeze([...NODE_DEPENDENCY_TREES, ...PYTHON_DEPENDENCY_TREES]);

const LOWER = new Set(DEPENDENCY_TREES.map((t) => t.toLowerCase()));

/** Whether a directory name is a dependency tree, in any case: `Node_Modules` lands in `node_modules` on a case-insensitive filesystem (D-R15-07). */
export function isDependencyTree(name: string): boolean {
  return LOWER.has(name.toLowerCase());
}

===== packages/adapters/test/python.test.ts =====
/**
 * The Python set (R29): which repositories get it, pytest's collection as
 * pytest 8.4.2 collects it (the expectations below are its `--collect-only`
 * output over the same files, D-R29-07), what it refuses, its runner, its
 * coverage, and its installers.
 */
import { mkdir, writeFile } from 'node:fs/promises';
import { join } from 'node:path';
import { afterEach, describe, expect, test } from 'vitest';
import {
  AdapterRefusal, buildAdapterSet, CoveragePyAdapter, isConfigFile, PIP_INSTALLER, PYTHON_DEPENDENCY_TREES, PYTHON_IMAGE, PytestAdapter, UV_INSTALLER,
} from '../src/index.js';
import { pkg, repo, cleanup } from './repo.js';

afterEach(cleanup);

const HASH = `sha256:${'a'.repeat(64)}`;

export function pyproject(extra = '', dev = '"pytest>=8"'): string {
  return `[project]\nname = "hello"\nversion = "0.1.0"\nrequires-python = ">=3.13"\n\n[dependency-groups]\ndev = [${dev}]\n${extra}`;
}

export function uvLock(version = '8.4.2'): string {
  return [
    'version = 1', 'revision = 3', 'requires-python = ">=3.13"', '',
    '[[package]]', 'name = "hello"', 'version = "0.1.0"', 'source = { virtual = "." }', '',
    '[[package]]', 'name = "pytest"', `version = "${version}"`, 'source = { registry = "https://pypi.org/simple" }',
    `wheels = [`, `    { url = "https://files.pythonhosted.org/p/pytest-${version}-py3-none-any.whl", hash = "${HASH}" },`, ']', '',
  ].join('\n');
}

const TEST_A = `import unittest
import pytest
from unittest import TestCase


@pytest.fixture
def test_fixture_not_a_case():
    return 1


@pytest.mark.parametrize("a", [1, 2.5, "x", None, True, -3, 1.0, (1, 2), [3]])
def test_single(a):
    assert a == a


@pytest.mark.parametrize("a,b", [(1, 2), ("q", "q")], ids=["one", None])
def test_multi(a, b):
    assert a != b


@pytest.mark.parametrize("v", [pytest.param(1, id="first"), pytest.param(2, marks=pytest.mark.skip), 1, 1])
def test_params(v):
    assert v


@pytest.mark.parametrize("x", [0, 1])
@pytest.mark.parametrize("y", ["a", "b"])
def test_stacked(x, y):
    assert x is not None


@pytest.mark.parametrize("s", ["L" * 3, "a" + "b"])
def test_nonliteral(s):
    assert s


@pytest.mark.skip(reason="x")
def test_skipped():
    pytest.skip("inner")


class TestPlain:
    def test_m(self):
        assert 1 < 2 <= 3

    def helper(self):
        pass

    class TestInner:
        def test_n(self):
            assert not False


class TestHasInit:
    def __init__(self):
        pass

    def test_never(self):
        pass


class TestBase:
    def test_base_m(self):
        pass


class TestChild(TestBase):
    def test_child_m(self):
        pass


class Checks(TestCase):
    def test_u(self):
        self.assertEqual(1, 1)
        self.assertAlmostEqual(1.0, 1.0, places=3)

    def check_not(self):
        pass

    @unittest.skip("no")
    def test_skipped_u(self):
        pass


class TestNotCollected:
    __test__ = False

    def test_x(self):
        pass


async def test_async():
    assert True
`;

/** A Python repository pinning pytest at `version`, with `files` beside it. */
async function python(files: Readonly<Record<string, string>> = {}, version = '8.4.2', extra = ''): Promise<string> {
  return repo({ 'pyproject.toml': pyproject(extra), 'uv.lock': uvLock(version), ...files });
}

async function pytestOf(root: string): Promise<PytestAdapter> {
  const set = await buildAdapterSet(root, { provider: null, coverage: null });
  if (!(set.test instanceof PytestAdapter)) throw new Error(`no pytest adapter: ${set.reasons.get('test') ?? ''}`);
  return set.test;
}

const rel = (root: string, files: readonly string[]): string[] => files.map((f) => f.slice(root.length + 1).replaceAll('\\', '/'));

describe('the Python set', () => {
  test('a repository declaring pytest, pinned by its lockfile, gets pytest@<major>, coverage.py, the manifest, Semgrep, its trees, and the Python image', async () => {
    const coverage = { report: '/r/coverage.json', sourceRoot: '/workspace' };
    const eight = await buildAdapterSet(await python(), { provider: null, coverage });
    const nine = await buildAdapterSet(await python({}, '9.2.0'), { provider: null, coverage: null });
    expect([eight.stack, nine.stack]).toEqual(['pytest@8', 'pytest@9']);
    expect(eight.coverage).toBeInstanceOf(CoveragePyAdapter);
    expect(eight.manifest).not.toBeNull();
    expect(eight.staticAnalysis?.kind).toBe('static-analysis');
    expect(eight.checkImage).toBe(PYTHON_IMAGE);
    expect(eight.dependencyTrees).toEqual(PYTHON_DEPENDENCY_TREES);
  });

  test('pytest pinned by a hashed requirements.txt is read from it', async () => {
    const root = await repo({ 'requirements.txt': `pytest==9.0.1 \\\n    --hash=${HASH}\n` });
    expect((await buildAdapterSet(root, { provider: null, coverage: null })).stack).toBe('pytest@9');
  });

  test.each([
    ['no pytest declared', { 'pyproject.toml': pyproject('', '"requests"'), 'uv.lock': uvLock() }, /pytest is not declared/],
    ['pytest 8.3', { 'pyproject.toml': pyproject(), 'uv.lock': uvLock('8.3.5') }, /pytest 8\.3\.5 is pinned/],
    ['pytest 7', { 'pyproject.toml': pyproject(), 'uv.lock': uvLock('7.4.4') }, /pytest 7\.4\.4 is pinned/],
    ['no lockfile', { 'pyproject.toml': pyproject() }, /no lockfile pins its version/],
  ])('%s: test is null, the reason named, and no other runner stands in', async (_name, files, reason) => {
    const set = await buildAdapterSet(await repo(files), { provider: null, coverage: null });
    expect(set.test).toBeNull();
    expect(set.reasons.get('test')).toMatch(reason);
    expect(set.unavailableControls()).toContain('test');
  });

  test('a repository declaring both a Node.js and a Python framework gets neither', async () => {
    const set = await buildAdapterSet(await python({ 'package.json': pkg({ vitest: '4' }) }), { provider: null, coverage: null });
    expect(set.test).toBeNull();
    expect(set.reasons.get('test')).toMatch(/both vitest@4 and pytest@8 are declared/);
  });

  test('a repository declaring neither stack names both gaps and is refused at L3', async () => {
    const set = await buildAdapterSet(await repo({ 'README.md': '' }), { provider: null, coverage: null });
    expect(set.stack).toBe('unsupported');
    expect(set.unavailableControls()).toEqual(expect.arrayContaining(['test', 'manifest', 'static-analysis']));
  });
});

describe('pytest collection', () => {
  const LAYOUT = {
    'tests/test_a.py': TEST_A,
    'tests/sub/b_test.py': 'import pytest\n\ndef test_b():\n    assert 1 == pytest.approx(1.0, rel=1e-3)\n',
    'tests/helper.py': 'def test_h():\n    pass\n',
    'test_root.py': 'def test_root():\n    pass\n',
    'build/test_x.py': 'def test_build():\n    pass\n',
    'env/pyvenv.cfg': 'home = /usr\n',
    'env/test_env.py': 'def test_env():\n    pass\n',
    '.venv/test_venv.py': 'def test_venv():\n    pass\n',
    'pkg/__pycache__/test_cached.py': 'def test_c():\n    pass\n',
  };

  test('testpaths, python_files, norecursedirs, virtualenvs, and __pycache__ decide the suites, as pytest decides them', async () => {
    const withPaths = await python(LAYOUT, '8.4.2', '\n[tool.pytest.ini_options]\ntestpaths = ["tests"]\n');
    expect(rel(withPaths, await (await pytestOf(withPaths)).enumerateSuites(withPaths))).toEqual(['tests/sub/b_test.py', 'tests/test_a.py']);
    const withoutPaths = await python(LAYOUT);
    expect(rel(withoutPaths, await (await pytestOf(withoutPaths)).enumerateSuites(withoutPaths))).toEqual(['test_root.py', 'tests/sub/b_test.py', 'tests/test_a.py']);
  });

  test('python_files from pytest.ini, which pytest reads before pyproject.toml', async () => {
    const root = await python({ ...LAYOUT, 'pytest.ini': '[pytest]\npython_files = helper.py\ntestpaths = tests\n' }, '8.4.2', '\n[tool.pytest.ini_options]\ntestpaths = ["nowhere"]\n');
    expect(rel(root, await (await pytestOf(root)).enumerateSuites(root))).toEqual(['tests/helper.py']);
  });

  test("pytest 9's pytest.toml is read; pytest 8 passes over it", async () => {
    const files = { ...LAYOUT, 'pytest.toml': '[pytest]\ntestpaths = ["tests/sub"]\n' };
    const nine = await python(files, '9.2.0');
    expect(rel(nine, await (await pytestOf(nine)).enumerateSuites(nine))).toEqual(['tests/sub/b_test.py']);
    const eight = await python(files, '8.4.2');
    expect(rel(eight, await (await pytestOf(eight)).enumerateSuites(eight))).toContain('test_root.py');
  });

  test('a named file is selected whatever its name; one that is not a file is not', async () => {
    const root = await python(LAYOUT);
    const adapter = await pytestOf(root);
    expect(await adapter.selectSuites(root, ['tests/helper.py', 'tests/test_a.py', 'tests/absent.py'])).toEqual(['tests/helper.py', 'tests/test_a.py']);
  });

  test('cases are the ones pytest 8.4.2 collects, with a non-literal table counted as its bare name', async () => {
    const root = await python(LAYOUT);
    const cases = await (await pytestOf(root)).enumerateCases(join(root, 'tests', 'test_a.py'));
    expect([...cases].sort()).toEqual([
      'Checks > test_skipped_u', 'Checks > test_u',
      'TestBase > test_base_m', 'TestChild > test_base_m', 'TestChild > test_child_m',
      'TestPlain > TestInner > test_n', 'TestPlain > test_m',
      'test_async',
      'test_multi[one]', 'test_multi[q-q]',
      'test_nonliteral',
      'test_params[1_0]', 'test_params[1_1]', 'test_params[2]', 'test_params[first]',
      'test_single[-3]', 'test_single[1.0]', 'test_single[1]', 'test_single[2.5]', 'test_single[None]', 'test_single[True]', 'test_single[a7]', 'test_single[a8]', 'test_single[x]',
      'test_skipped',
      'test_stacked[a-0]', 'test_stacked[a-1]', 'test_stacked[b-0]', 'test_stacked[b-1]',
    ].sort());
  });

  test('skip markers: pytest marks and calls, unittest decorators, a skipped param, and pytestmark', async () => {
    const root = await python({ ...LAYOUT, 'tests/test_m.py': 'import pytest\npytestmark = pytest.mark.skip\n\ndef test_z():\n    pass\n' });
    const adapter = await pytestOf(root);
    expect([...(await adapter.detectSkipMarkers(join(root, 'tests', 'test_a.py')))].sort()).toEqual([
      'pytest.mark.skip: test_skipped',
      'pytest.param(marks=mark.skip): test_params[2]',
      'pytest.skip(): test_skipped',
      'unittest.skip: Checks > test_skipped_u',
    ]);
    expect(await adapter.detectSkipMarkers(join(root, 'tests', 'test_m.py'))).toEqual([`pytestmark pytest.mark.skip: ${join(root, 'tests', 'test_m.py')}`]);
  });

  test('a file the grammar cannot read is refused, never read in part', async () => {
    const root = await python({ 'tests/test_bad.py': 'def test_x(:\n    pass\n' });
    await expect((await pytestOf(root)).enumerateCases(join(root, 'tests', 'test_bad.py'))).rejects.toThrow(/does not parse/);
  });
});

describe('what pytest collection refuses', () => {
  test.each([
    ['a conftest collection hook', { 'tests/conftest.py': 'def pytest_collection_modifyitems(items):\n    items.clear()\n' }, '', /pytest_collection_modifyitems/],
    ['a conftest that loads plugins', { 'conftest.py': 'pytest_plugins = ["x"]\n' }, '', /pytest_plugins/],
    ['a conftest that ignores paths', { 'conftest.py': 'collect_ignore = ["tests"]\n' }, '', /collect_ignore/],
    ['a pytest_configure that does more than register markers', { 'conftest.py': 'def pytest_configure(config):\n    config.option.keyword = "x"\n' }, '', /pytest_configure/],
    ['a pytest.ini below the root', { 'tests/pytest.ini': '' }, '', /configures pytest below the root/],
    ['addopts that select', {}, '\n[tool.pytest.ini_options]\naddopts = "-k fast"\n', /addopts passes -k/],
    ['a required plugin', {}, '\n[tool.pytest.ini_options]\nrequired_plugins = "pytest-xdist"\n', /requires plugins/],
    ['an ini value with a comment character', { 'pytest.ini': '[pytest]\ntestpaths = tests # the suite\n' }, '', /comment character/],
  ])('%s', async (_name, files, extra, reason) => {
    const set = await buildAdapterSet(await python({ 'tests/test_a.py': 'def test_a():\n    pass\n', ...files }, '8.4.2', extra), { provider: null, coverage: null });
    expect(set.test).toBeNull();
    expect(set.reasons.get('test')).toMatch(reason);
  });

  test('a pytest_configure that only registers markers, and output-only addopts, are read', async () => {
    const root = await python(
      { 'conftest.py': 'def pytest_configure(config):\n    """Markers."""\n    config.addinivalue_line("markers", "slow: slow")\n' },
      '8.4.2', '\n[tool.pytest.ini_options]\naddopts = "-ra -q --strict-markers --tb=short"\n',
    );
    expect((await buildAdapterSet(root, { provider: null, coverage: null })).test).not.toBeNull();
  });
});

describe('the pytest runner', () => {
  test('the install\'s interpreter, no plugin autoloaded, no doctest, no cache, each named test ./-led', async () => {
    const adapter = await pytestOf(await python());
    expect(adapter.runner).toBe('.venv/bin/python');
    expect(adapter.invocation(['tests/-x.py'])).toEqual(['.venv/bin/python', '-m', 'pytest', '--disable-plugin-autoload', '-p', 'no:cacheprovider', '-p', 'no:doctest', './tests/-x.py']);
    expect(adapter.invocation(null)).toHaveLength(8);
  });

  test('missingRunner names a missing interpreter, then a missing pytest, and is null when both are installed', async () => {
    const root = await python();
    const adapter = await pytestOf(root);
    expect(await adapter.missingRunner(root)).toMatch(/interpreter is not installed at \.venv\/bin\/python/);
    await mkdir(join(root, '.venv', 'bin'), { recursive: true });
    await writeFile(join(root, '.venv', 'bin', 'python'), '');
    expect(await adapter.missingRunner(root)).toMatch(/pytest@8 is not installed in \.venv/);
    await mkdir(join(root, '.venv', 'lib', 'python3.13', 'site-packages', 'pytest'), { recursive: true });
    await writeFile(join(root, '.venv', 'lib', 'python3.13', 'site-packages', 'pytest', '__init__.py'), '');
    expect(await adapter.missingRunner(root)).toBeNull();
  });
});

describe('Python assertions', () => {
  const BEFORE = `import pytest

def test_x():
    assert total == 5
    assert x == pytest.approx(1.0, rel=1e-6)
    with pytest.raises(ValueError):
        f()
    assert ready
    assert gone == 1
`;
  const AFTER = `import pytest

def test_x():
    assert total != 5
    assert x == pytest.approx(1.0, rel=0.1)
    with pytest.raises(ValueError, match="bad"):
        f()
    assert ready == 1
`;

  test('a flipped comparison is weakened, a wider approx is widened, a removed one is removed, and a stronger form is not reported', async () => {
    const root = await python({ 'tests/before.py': BEFORE, 'tests/after.py': AFTER });
    const adapter = await pytestOf(root);
    const before = await adapter.parseAssertions(join(root, 'tests', 'before.py'));
    const after = await adapter.parseAssertions(join(root, 'tests', 'after.py'));
    expect(before.map((a) => [a.operator, ...a.args])).toEqual([
      ['==', 'total', '5'], ['== approx', 'x', '1.0'], ['raises', 'ValueError'], ['truthy', 'ready'], ['==', 'gone', '1'],
    ]);
    const delta = adapter.compareAssertions(before, after);
    expect(delta.weakened.map((a) => adapter.spell(a))).toEqual(['line 4: assert total != 5']);
    expect(delta.toleranceWidened.map((a) => a.tolerance)).toEqual([0.1]);
    expect(delta.removed.map((a) => adapter.spell(a))).toEqual(['line 9: assert gone == 1']);
  });

  test('unittest assertions and almost-equal places become a comparable band', async () => {
    const root = await python({ 'tests/u.py': 'class T:\n    def test(self):\n        self.assertAlmostEqual(a, 1.0, places=3)\n        self.assertIn(x, y, msg="m")\n' });
    const found = await (await pytestOf(root)).parseAssertions(join(root, 'tests', 'u.py'));
    expect(found.map((a) => [a.operator, a.args, a.tolerance])).toEqual([['assertAlmostEqual', ['a', '1.0'], 0.0005], ['assertIn', ['x', 'y'], undefined]]);
  });
});

describe('coverage.py', () => {
  test('a changed statement the report counts as missing is uncovered, one it never names still counts, and a test file is excluded', async () => {
    const base = await python({ 'hello/__init__.py': 'def greet(n):\n    return "hi " + n\n', 'tests/test_h.py': 'def test_h():\n    pass\n' });
    const head = await python({
      'hello/__init__.py': 'def greet(n):\n    """Doc."""\n    if n:\n        return "hello " + n\n    return "none"  # pragma: no cover\n',
      'tests/test_h.py': 'def test_h():\n    assert True\n',
    });
    const report = join(head, 'coverage.json');
    await writeFile(report, JSON.stringify({ meta: { format: 3 }, files: { 'hello/__init__.py': { executed_lines: [1, 3, 4], missing_lines: [], excluded_lines: [5] } } }));
    const tests = await pytestOf(head);
    const adapter = new CoveragePyAdapter({ report, sourceRoot: '/workspace', tests });
    // Changed: 2 (docstring, not a statement), 3, 4 covered, 5 a statement the report excluded: 2 of 3.
    expect(await adapter.changedLineCoverage(base, head)).toBeCloseTo(2 / 3);
  });

  test('a report that is not coverage.py JSON is refused', async () => {
    const root = await python();
    await writeFile(join(root, 'c.json'), JSON.stringify({ totals: {} }));
    const adapter = new CoveragePyAdapter({ report: join(root, 'c.json'), sourceRoot: '/workspace', tests: await pytestOf(root) });
    await expect(adapter.changedLineCoverage(root, root)).rejects.toBeInstanceOf(AdapterRefusal);
  });
});

describe('the Python installers', () => {
  test('each makes a virtualenv with copies first, builds nothing, installs no project, and verifies every hash', () => {
    for (const installer of [UV_INSTALLER, PIP_INSTALLER]) {
      expect(installer.steps[0]).toEqual(['python', '-m', 'venv', '--copies', '.venv']);
      expect(installer.image).toBe(PYTHON_IMAGE);
      expect(installer.trees).toEqual(PYTHON_DEPENDENCY_TREES);
    }
    expect(UV_INSTALLER.steps[1]).toEqual(expect.arrayContaining(['uv', 'sync', '--frozen', '--no-install-workspace', '--no-build']));
    expect(PIP_INSTALLER.steps[1]).toEqual(expect.arrayContaining(['--require-hashes', '--no-deps', '--only-binary', ':all:']));
    expect(PIP_INSTALLER.env.PIP_CONFIG_FILE).toBe('/dev/null');
  });

  test('uv.lock: a registry wheel is verifiable; the project is skipped; a git source, a package with no wheel, and an unhashed wheel are not', () => {
    const lock = [
      uvLock(),
      '[[package]]', 'name = "fromgit"', 'version = "1.0"', 'source = { git = "https://example.com/x.git" }', '',
      '[[package]]', 'name = "sdistonly"', 'version = "1.0"', 'source = { registry = "https://pypi.org/simple" }', 'sdist = { url = "https://x/s.tar.gz", hash = "sha256:00" }', '',
      '[[package]]', 'name = "nohash"', 'version = "1.0"', 'source = { registry = "https://pypi.org/simple" }', 'wheels = [{ url = "https://x/n.whl" }]', '',
    ].join('\n');
    const read = UV_INSTALLER.packages(lock);
    if (!read.ok) throw new Error(read.reason);
    expect(read.packages.map((p) => [p.key, p.unverifiable === null ? null : p.unverifiable.split(',')[0]])).toEqual([
      ['fromgit@1.0', 'it comes from git'],
      ['nohash@1.0', 'a wheel of it records no sha256 hash'],
      ['pytest@8.4.2', null],
      ['sdistonly@1.0', 'it has no wheel'],
    ]);
  });

  test('requirements.txt: a pinned, hashed line is verifiable; an unpinned or unhashed one is not; an option line is refused', () => {
    const read = PIP_INSTALLER.packages(`pytest==8.4.2 \\\n    --hash=${HASH}\nrequests>=2 --hash=${HASH}\nidna==3.0\n`);
    if (!read.ok) throw new Error(read.reason);
    expect(read.packages.map((p) => [p.key, p.unverifiable])).toEqual([
      ['idna@3.0', 'it records no hash'], ['pytest@8.4.2', null], ['requests@(unpinned)', 'it is not pinned to one version with =='],
    ]);
    const option = PIP_INSTALLER.packages(`--index-url https://evil.example/simple\npytest==8.4.2 --hash=${HASH}\n`);
    expect(option.ok ? 'read' : option.reason).toMatch(/option --index-url/);
  });

  test('a failed install names a hash failure, a missing wheel, or an unreachable registry', () => {
    expect(UV_INSTALLER.failure('error: Hash mismatch for `iniconfig==2.3.1`')).toMatch(/failed the hash/);
    expect(PIP_INSTALLER.failure('ERROR: THESE PACKAGES DO NOT MATCH THE HASHES FROM THE REQUIREMENTS FILE.')).toMatch(/failed the hash/);
    expect(UV_INSTALLER.failure('Building source distributions is disabled')).toMatch(/no wheel/);
    expect(UV_INSTALLER.failure('error: Failed to fetch: `https://pypi.org/simple/x/`')).toMatch(/could not be reached/);
  });
});

describe("Python's config files", () => {
  test.each(['pyproject.toml', 'uv.lock', 'requirements-dev.txt', 'tests/conftest.py', 'pytest.ini', 'setup.cfg', '.coveragerc', 'pkg/x.pth', 'sitecustomize.py'])('%s is a config file', (path) => {
    expect(isConfigFile(path)).toBe(true);
  });

  test('a test file and a module are not', () => {
    expect(isConfigFile('tests/test_a.py')).toBe(false);
    expect(isConfigFile('hello/__init__.py')).toBe(false);
  });
});

   ctx: LineContext, plan: Extract<InstallPlan, { kind: 'install' }>, inputs: readonly string[], tree: string, base: string, dir: string, taskId: TaskId | null, station: StationId,
 ): Promise<{ ok: true; install: InstallRecord } | { ok: false; at: 'provision' | 'install'; reason: string }> {
   const { installer } = plan;
   const { sandbox, workspaces, profile, vault } = ctx.components;
   // Built beside its final place and renamed in, so two tasks installing the same inputs at once never share a directory.

   const work = join(building, ROOT);
   await mkdir(work, { recursive: true, mode: 0o700 });

     await mkdir(dirname(target), { recursive: true });
     await copyFile(join(tree, ...path.split('/')), target);

     user: { ...workspaces.user },
   };
   let handle: SandboxHandle;
   try {
     handle = await sandbox.provision(spec);
   } catch (error) {

+    // Each step in turn, in the one sandbox, stopping at the first that fails (A-R29-02).
+    for (const [index, step] of installer.steps.entries()) {
+      const exec = await sandbox.exec(handle, [...step], { env: { ...installer.env } });
+      if (exec.exitCode === 0) continue;
+      const cause = installer.failure(`${exec.stdout}\n${exec.stderr}`) ?? `step ${String(index + 1)} of ${String(installer.steps.length)} (${step[0]}) exited ${String(exec.exitCode)}`;
       failed = `the install from ${installer.lockfile} failed: ${cause}`;
+      break;
     }
   } catch (error) {
     failed = `the install from ${installer.lockfile} did not run: ${error instanceof Error ? error.message : String(error)}`;
   } finally {
     // Whatever the install's proxy decided is recorded, as every sandbox's is (D-P14-04).
     const { egress } = await sandbox.destroy(handle);
     await recordEgress({ vault, runId: ctx.run.id, taskId, station }, egress);
   }
   if (failed !== undefined) {
     await rm(building, { recursive: true, force: true });
     return { ok: false, at: 'install', reason: failed };
   }
 
   const trees = await dependencyTrees(work, treeNamer([installer]));
   const escaped = await linkOut(work, trees);
   if (escaped !== null) {
     await rm(building, { recursive: true, force: true });
     return { ok: false, at: 'install', reason: `the install from ${installer.lockfile} wrote ${escaped}, a link out of its dependency trees, so a check would load code the lockfile's hashes do not cover` };
   }
   const baseText = (await isFile(join(base, installer.lockfile))) ? await readFile(join(base, installer.lockfile), 'utf8') : null;

     stack: installer.stack,

+    steps: installer.steps.map((step) => [...step]),

     installedTreeSha256: await installedDigest(work, trees),
     collectedBy: 'runtime',
   };

   try {
     await rename(building, dir);
   } catch {

     await rm(building, { recursive: true, force: true });
     if (!(await isFile(join(dir, RECORD)))) throw new Error(`install: ${dir} was neither made by this install nor by another`);
     return { ok: true, install: JSON.parse(await readFile(join(dir, RECORD), 'utf8')) as InstallRecord };

+  const stackImage = await checkImageOf(basePath(workspaces, ctx.run.id));
   const security = ctx.checks.filter(isSecurityCheck);
   const tamper = await analyzeTamper(prior.tree, tree, {
     protectedPaths: ctx.policy.protectedPaths,
     coverage: coverageReport(ctx),
     // A security check pins no command: its scanner's arguments are the runtime's, and name no file in the tree.
     commands: ctx.checks.filter((c) => !isSecurityCheck(c)).map((c) => commandOf(c, runner)),
     markers: [...new Set(security.map((c) => c.kind))].map((kind) => ({ kind, markers: scannerFor(kind, ctx.components.scanners).markers })),
   });
 
   // Results stay aligned with the specs by position, never matched up by id afterwards.
   const specs = ctx.checks;
   const results: Array<CheckResult | undefined> = [];
   const unstarted: UnstartedCheck[] = [];
   // Each check gets a sandbox of its own, whose wall clock is the check's pinned timeout. A check
   // shares no container with the one before it, so nothing an earlier check did to the container
   // can change what a later one runs (codex-2); and a check that outruns its timeout is ended by
   // the provider and produces no result, recorded in `unstarted` like any other (codex-7).
   for (const [index, check] of specs.entries()) {
     if (isSecurityCheck(check)) {
       const scanned = await runSecurity(ctx, tree, diffSha256, index, check, task.id);
       if (scanned.ok) results.push(scanned.result);
       else if (scanned.at === 'provision') return spendRetry(ctx, task);
       else {
         results.push(undefined);
         unstarted.push({ checkId: check.id, reason: scanned.reason });
       }
       continue;
     }
     // With the install refused or failed, the tree holds no dependency tree at all, so no check runs over it as if it did (I5).
     if (!installed.ok) {
       results.push(undefined);
       unstarted.push({ checkId: check.id, reason: installed.reason });
       continue;
     }
     const missing = await runnerMissing(check, runner, tree);
     if (missing !== undefined) {
       results.push(undefined);
       unstarted.push({ checkId: check.id, reason: missing });
       continue;
     }
     let handle: SandboxHandle;
     try {
-      handle = await sandbox.provision(checkSandbox(ctx, tree, check));
+      handle = await sandbox.provision(checkSandbox(ctx, tree, check, stackImage));
     } catch {
       return spendRetry(ctx, task);
     }
     try {
       const startedAt = new Date().toISOString();
       try {
         // The pinned argument vector, or a suite check's runner, exactly: no shell, no splitting (A-P6-01, D-R15-04).
         const exec = await sandbox.exec(handle, [...commandOf(check, runner)]);
         results.push({
           checkId: check.id,
           exitCode: exec.exitCode,
           stdout: exec.stdout,
           stderr: exec.stderr,
           suiteCount: await suitesRun(check, runner, tree),
           expectation: null,
           findings: null,
           durationMs: exec.durationMs,
           startedAt,
         });
       } catch (error) {
         // There is no exit code to record and none is invented; the bundle says why (A-P6-02).
         results.push(undefined);
         unstarted.push({ checkId: check.id, reason: describe(error) });
       }
     } finally {
       // A check's sandbox has no route out today, and whatever its proxy decided is recorded all the same (D-P14-04).
       const { egress } = await sandbox.destroy(handle);
       await recordEgress({ vault: ctx.components.vault, runId: ctx.run.id, taskId: task.id, station: ctx.state.station }, egress);
     }
   }
   const checks = results.filter((r): r is CheckResult => r !== undefined);
 
   // Evidence collected over a changed artifact is not evidence, whatever the provider claims about ro.
   const after = [...(await tamperedPaths(ctx)), ...(await tamperedIn(ctx, tree))];
   if (after.length > 0) return recordTamper(ctx, 'verify', task, after, { phase: 'after-checks', checks });
 
   // The `test-design` exit is not crossed without a base record, so a task at verify has one (D-A-BF-01).
   const baseRun = await vault.readBaseRun(ctx.run.id, testDraftOf(ctx));
   if (baseRun === null) throw new Error(`line: run ${ctx.run.id} reached verify with no base record`);
   const baseRecord = await readRecord<BaseRunRecord>(ctx, baseRun);
   const failed: FailedCheck[] = [];
   specs.forEach((check, i) => {
     // A security check is judged by its findings against the base's, never by its exit code alone (D-A-SC-02).
     const shortfall = isSecurityCheck(check)
       ? securityShortfall(check, results[i], baseRecord.checks.find((r) => r.checkId === check.id)?.findings ?? [], scannerFor(check.kind, ctx.components.scanners), tamper)
       : requiredShortfall(check, results[i]);
     if (shortfall !== undefined) failed.push(shortfall);
   });
   const differences = claimEvidenceDiff(result.claim, own);
   if (differences.length > 0) {
     // Recorded, and deliberately not entered in run state: every entry there halts the run, and a
     // halt the claim could trigger would let the model's story decide the outcome (D-P6-03).
     await recordViolation(ctx, {
       runId: ctx.run.id,
       taskId: task.id,
       kind: 'claim-mismatch',
       role: task.role,
       driverProvenanceId: driver.provenanceId(),
       contractVersion: driver.contractVersion,
       detectedAt: new Date().toISOString(),
       detail: { station: 'verify', claimEvidenceDiff: differences },
     });
   }
   await recordFindings(ctx, task, tamper);
   const evidence: EvidenceBundle = {
     runId: ctx.run.id,
     taskId: task.id,
     baseCommit: ctx.run.baseCommit,
     baseTreeSha256: ctx.baseTreeSha256,
     baseRun,
     diff,
     diffSha256,
     checks,
     unstarted,
     claim: result.claim,
     claimEvidenceDiff: differences,
     tamper,
     notCarried,
     install: installed.ok ? installed.install : null,
     collectedBy: 'runtime',
     driverProvenanceId: driver.provenanceId(),
     contractVersion: driver.contractVersion,
   };
   const ref = await vault.writeEvidence(evidence);
   // The status follows the verdict and nothing else (I2). A failure with no iteration left parks the task;
   // an iteration a conflict sent back was judged by no gate and spends none of them (D-R4-03).
   const spent = gatedIterations(attemptsOf(ctx.state, task));
   const status: TaskStatus = failed.length === 0 ? 'passed' : spent >= STATION_CONTRACTS[task.station].maxIterations ? 'parked' : 'failed';
   // A failed gate is a decision, recorded before the status it sets is committed, at every iteration, so each failure is counted by its cause (R17, D-R17-06).
   if (failed.length > 0) {
     await recordDecision(
       { vault, runId: ctx.run.id, taskId: task.id, station: 'verify' },
       { cause: 'station-refused', decidedBy: 'line', refusal: { ok: false, reason: 'gate-failed', failed, message: `the gate failed on ${failed.map((f) => `${f.checkId} (${f.cause})`).join(', ')}` } },
     );
   }
   // The station machine returns this park to the line on its next step; it is recorded here, first.
   if (status === 'parked') await park(ctx, task, { evidence: ref }, 'iterations-exhausted');
   else await commit(ctx, { tasks: { [task.id]: status }, evidence: ref });
   return undefined;
 }
 
 /**
  * A review seat's tree: the files its contract's grants cover and nothing
  * else. The review contract grants the locked spec, the acceptance tests, and
  * the diff, so the view holds those files as the accepted tree has them; the
  * security seat's contract does not grant the acceptance tests, so its view
  * holds none (R9, codex-3). The task graph, the manifest, and every file the
  * diff does not carry are absent, so a reviewer with a read tool cannot open
  * what its contract denies it (I6).
  */

 export function readinessScanner(options: ReadinessScannerOptions): ReadinessScanner {

   if (profile === null) throw new Error('readiness: the host has no sandbox profile, so a scan has no image to run in');
   return {
     resolve: (revision) => resolveCommit(options.repository, revision),
     scan: async (commit) => {

     (await test.enumerateSuites(root)).map((abs) => ({ abs, rel: relative(root, abs).replaceAll('\\', '/') }));
   const [baseTests, headTests] = await Promise.all([listed(base), listed(head)]);
   const baseSet = new Set(baseTests.map((f) => f.rel));

   // A file unchanged and a test on both sides contributes the same to each side, so only the rest
   // is read. A file that stopped being a test, by a config change, is read on the side it still is.
   const before = baseTests.filter((f) => changed.has(f.rel) || !headSet.has(f.rel));
   const after = headTests.filter((f) => changed.has(f.rel) || !baseSet.has(f.rel));
 
   const withFile = (entries: Array<{ file: string; item: Assertion }>): Assertion[] => entries.map(({ file, item }) => ({ ...item, file }));

-    ...delta.removed.map((b) => ({ file: b.file, before: spell(b), after: '(removed)' })),
+    ...[...delta.weakened, ...delta.toleranceWidened].map((a) => ({ file: a.file, before: former(a), after: test.spell(a) })),
+    ...delta.removed.map((b) => ({ file: b.file, before: test.spell(b), after: '(removed)' })),
   ];
 
   // A marker pairs within its file, and across files only from a file that left the tests to one
   // that joined them — a rename or a move. Between two files that both survive, a marker is not the
   // same marker: `.only` moved from one to the other focuses a different set of tests.
   const markersBefore = await read(before, (abs) => test.detectSkipMarkers(abs));
   const markersAfter = await read(after, (abs) => test.detectSkipMarkers(abs));
   const unmatched = markersAfter.filter((m) => take(markersBefore, (b) => b.file === m.file && b.item === m.item) === undefined);

     .map(({ file, item }) => ({ file, marker: item }));

   }
   return { assertionsWeakened, skipMarkersAdded, testsDeleted };
 }

     expect(lockedPackages(NPM_INSTALLER, '{ not json')).toMatchObject({ ok: false });
     expect(lockedPackages(NPM_INSTALLER, JSON.stringify({ lockfileVersion: 3 }))).toMatchObject({ ok: false });
   });
 
   test('pnpm: a registry package is its integrity; a tarball, git, or directory resolution cannot be verified', () => {
     const read = lockedPackages(PNPM_INSTALLER, [
       "lockfileVersion: '9.0'",
       'packages:',
       '  a@1.0.0:',
       `    resolution: {integrity: ${HASH}}`,
       '  evil@file:evil-1.0.0.tgz:',
       `    resolution: {integrity: ${HASH}, tarball: file:evil-1.0.0.tgz}`,
       '  g@https://codeload.github.com/x/y/tar.gz/abc:',
       '    resolution: {tarball: https://codeload.github.com/x/y/tar.gz/abc}',
       '  r@git+https://example.com/r.git#abc:',
       '    resolution: {commit: abc, repo: https://example.com/r.git, type: git}',
       '',
     ].join('\n'));
     expect(read).toEqual({
       ok: true,
       packages: [
         { key: 'a@1.0.0', integrity: HASH, unverifiable: null },
         { key: 'evil@file:evil-1.0.0.tgz', integrity: HASH, unverifiable: 'it resolves from file:evil-1.0.0.tgz, not from a registry' },
         { key: 'g@https://codeload.github.com/x/y/tar.gz/abc', integrity: null, unverifiable: 'it records no integrity hash' },
         { key: 'r@git+https://example.com/r.git#abc', integrity: null, unverifiable: 'it is a git dependency, not a registry download' },
       ],
     });
   });
 
   test('pnpm: another lockfile version is refused; a lockfile with no packages pins none', () => {
     expect(lockedPackages(PNPM_INSTALLER, "lockfileVersion: '6.0'\n")).toMatchObject({ ok: false, reason: expect.stringMatching(/6\.0/) as unknown });
     expect(lockedPackages(PNPM_INSTALLER, "lockfileVersion: '9.0'\nimporters: {}\n")).toEqual({ ok: true, packages: [] });
   });
 
   test('neither installer runs a lifecycle script or reads a pnpmfile, and each names a failed hash (D-R34-02)', () => {
-    expect(NPM_INSTALLER.invocation).toContain('--ignore-scripts');
+    expect(NPM_INSTALLER.steps).toEqual([expect.arrayContaining(['--ignore-scripts'])]);
     expect(NPM_INSTALLER.env.npm_config_ignore_scripts).toBe('true');
-    expect(PNPM_INSTALLER.invocation).toEqual(expect.arrayContaining(['--frozen-lockfile', '--ignore-scripts', '--ignore-pnpmfile']));
+    expect(PNPM_INSTALLER.steps).toEqual([expect.arrayContaining(['--frozen-lockfile', '--ignore-scripts', '--ignore-pnpmfile'])]);
     expect([...NPM_INSTALLER.files, ...PNPM_INSTALLER.files]).not.toEqual(expect.arrayContaining(['.npmrc']));
     expect([...NPM_INSTALLER.files, ...PNPM_INSTALLER.files]).not.toEqual(expect.arrayContaining(['.pnpmfile.cjs']));
     expect(NPM_INSTALLER.failure('npm error code EINTEGRITY')).toMatch(/integrity hash/);
     expect(PNPM_INSTALLER.failure('ERR_PNPM_TARBALL_INTEGRITY')).toMatch(/integrity hash/);
   });
 });

 export const UNSUPPORTED_STACK_IS_LOUD: LocalAssertion = runtime({
   id: 'I5.unsupported-stack-is-loud',
   title:
     'an adapter set names every control it lacks — each null slot and each behavioral kind it does not carry — and is ' +
     'refused at L3 naming each, derived from its slots even when its own unavailableControls() under-reports; L0-L2 are not refused',
   run: async () => {
     const { adapterAdmission, buildAdapterSet } = await import('@olympus-ai/adapters');
     const options = { provider: null, coverage: null };
 
     await withRepository({ 'package.json': manifest({ mocha: '10.0.0' }) }, async (root) => {
       const set = await buildAdapterSet(root, options);
       if (set.test !== null) throw new Error(`I5: a repository with no supported framework got a test adapter (${set.test.stack})`);
       const named = set.unavailableControls();
       for (const control of ['test', 'coverage', 'mutation', 'behavioral:cli', 'behavioral:http', 'behavioral:browser']) {
         if (!named.includes(control)) throw new Error(`I5: an unsupported stack did not name ${control} among [${named.join(', ')}]`);
       }
       const l3 = adapterAdmission(set, 3);
       if (l3.ok) throw new Error('I5: an unsupported stack was admitted at L3');
       if (!l3.unavailable.includes('test')) throw new Error(`I5: the L3 refusal did not name the missing test adapter: ${l3.message}`);
       for (const level of [0, 1, 2] as const) {
         const admitted = adapterAdmission(set, level);
         if (!admitted.ok || admitted.level !== level) throw new Error(`I5: L${String(level)} was refused or changed by the adapter set`);
       }
     });
 
     // A supported stack still lacks mutation (M3) and the HTTP and browser kinds, so L3 stays refused at M1.
     await withRepository({ 'package.json': manifest({ vitest: '^4.1.0' }) }, async (root) => {
       const set = await buildAdapterSet(root, options);
       if (set.test === null) throw new Error(`I5: a vitest repository got no test adapter: ${set.reasons.get('test') ?? ''}`);
       const l3 = adapterAdmission(set, 3);
       if (l3.ok) throw new Error('I5: a set with no mutation adapter was admitted at L3');
       for (const control of ['mutation', 'behavioral:http', 'behavioral:browser']) {
         if (!l3.unavailable.includes(control)) throw new Error(`I5: the L3 refusal did not name ${control}: ${l3.message}`);
       }
     });
 
     // The refusal reads the slots, not only the set's own report of itself.
     const underReporting = {
-      stack: 'custom', test: null, coverage: null, mutation: null, behavioral: [], manifest: null, staticAnalysis: null, unavailableControls: (): string[] => [],
+      stack: 'custom', test: null, coverage: null, mutation: null, behavioral: [], manifest: null, staticAnalysis: null, dependencyTrees: [], checkImage: null, unavailableControls: (): string[] => [],
     };
     const lied = adapterAdmission(underReporting, 3);
     if (lied.ok || !lied.unavailable.includes('mutation')) {
       throw new Error('I5: a set whose unavailableControls() returned nothing was admitted at L3 despite its null slots');
     }
   },
 });

 /** I3: An agent may not be judged by an artifact it can write. */
 export const I3: InvariantEntry = {
   title: INVARIANTS.I3,
   assertions: [
     // R24: the target repository's instructions as granted context.
     ...R24_I3,
     // R20: the role compiler.
     // R19: drafting at spec and test-design.
     ...R19_ASSERTIONS.I3,
     ...R16_ASSERTIONS.I3,
     ...R8B_I3,
     ...R20_I3,

     ...R23_I3,
     // R17: security at the gate.
     ...R17_I3,
     // R4: parallel task execution.
     ...R4_I3,
     compileError({
       id: 'I3.context-grants-name-locked-artifacts',
       title: 'ContextGrant names the locked spec and acceptance tests; there is no grant for a mutable spec',
       fixture: 'i3/context-grants-name-locked-artifacts.ts',
     }),
     compileError({
       id: 'I3.test-design-context-is-the-locked-spec',
       title: 'the station contract table grants test-design exactly the locked spec: adding acceptance tests, the plan, or the repository, or granting nothing, does not compile',
       fixture: 'i3/test-design-context-is-the-locked-spec.ts',
     }),
     compileError({
       id: 'I3.lock-verdict-names-tampered-paths',
       title: 'a failed LockVerdict must list the tampered paths, and a passing one cannot carry a warning',
       fixture: 'i3/lock-verdict-names-tampered-paths.ts',
     }),
     runtime({
       id: 'I3.lock-verification-detects-change',
       title:
         'a locked artifact whose bytes change verifies as tampered, naming the path with the hash the manifest holds and the hash on disk; a deleted one is reported the same way rather than throwing; an untouched one verifies clean',
       run: async () => {
         await withVault('p1-i3-change-', async (vault, dirs) => {
           const runId = 'i3-change' as RunId;
           const spec = join(dirs.artifacts, 'spec.md');
           await writeFile(spec, '# hello\n\nThe capability, as locked.\n');
 
           const manifest = await vault.lock(runId, ['spec.md'], 'spec');
           const locked = entryFor(manifest.entries, 'spec.md');
 
           const intact = await vault.verifyLocks(runId);
           if (!intact.ok) {
             throw new Error(`I3: an untouched locked artifact verified as tampered (${JSON.stringify(intact.tampered)})`);
           }
 
           // The failure this invariant exists for: the artifact an agent is
           // judged by is edited after it was locked.
           await writeFile(spec, '# hello\n\nThe capability, quietly widened.\n');
           const changed = soleTampered(await vault.verifyLocks(runId), 'a rewritten locked artifact');
           if (changed.path !== 'spec.md') throw new Error(`I3: the tampered record named ${changed.path}, expected spec.md`);
           if (changed.expected !== locked.sha256) {
             throw new Error(`I3: the tampered record's expected hash is ${changed.expected}, not the ${locked.sha256} the manifest locked`);
           }
           if (changed.actual === changed.expected) {
             throw new Error('I3: the tampered record reports the same hash for expected and actual, so nothing was re-read from disk');
           }
 
           // A deleted artifact is a mismatch, not an exception: the line must
           // record the violation and refuse, exactly as it does for an edit.
           await rm(spec);
           const deleted = soleTampered(await vault.verifyLocks(runId), 'a deleted locked artifact');
           if (deleted.actual === changed.actual) {
             throw new Error('I3: a deleted locked artifact reports the same actual hash as an edited one; a deletion must be distinguishable');
           }
           if (deleted.expected !== locked.sha256) {
             throw new Error(`I3: the deleted artifact's expected hash is ${deleted.expected}, not the ${locked.sha256} the manifest locked`);
           }
         });
       },
     }),
     runtime({
       id: 'I3.lock-preserves-earlier-entries',
       title:
         'a later lock preserves every earlier entry, so a spec locked at `spec` is still verified after `test-design` locks the acceptance tests, and a re-lock cannot rebase an already-locked artifact\'s hash',
       run: async () => {
         await withVault('p1-i3-preserve-', async (vault, dirs) => {
           const runId = 'i3-preserve' as RunId;
           const spec = join(dirs.artifacts, 'spec.md');
           await writeFile(spec, '# hello\n\nThe capability, as locked.\n');
           await writeFile(join(dirs.artifacts, 'acceptance.test.ts'), "test('hello', () => { expect(run()).toBe(0); });\n");
 
           const first = await vault.lock(runId, ['spec.md'], 'spec');
           const lockedSpec = entryFor(first.entries, 'spec.md');
 
           // The second station locks its own artifact. Replace semantics would
           // drop the spec here, and I3 requires both locked and re-verified at
           // every transition.
           const second = await vault.lock(runId, ['acceptance.test.ts'], 'test-design');
           const preserved = entryFor(second.entries, 'spec.md');
           entryFor(second.entries, 'acceptance.test.ts');
           if (preserved.sha256 !== lockedSpec.sha256) {
             throw new Error(`I3: the second lock changed the spec's hash from ${lockedSpec.sha256} to ${preserved.sha256}`);
           }
           if (preserved.lockedBy !== 'spec') {
             throw new Error(`I3: the second lock rewrote the spec's station to ${preserved.lockedBy}; the station that locked an artifact is the audit record`);
           }
 
           // Verification after the second lock must still cover the first's entries.
           await writeFile(spec, '# hello\n\nThe capability, quietly widened.\n');
           const tampered = soleTampered(await vault.verifyLocks(runId), 'a spec edited after a later station locked its own artifact');
           if (tampered.path !== 'spec.md') {
             throw new Error(`I3: after the second lock, verification named ${tampered.path}; the spec locked at \`spec\` is no longer covered`);
           }
 
           // Re-locking is how a tamper would launder itself: lock the edited
           // file again and the manifest agrees with the disk.
           let rebased = true;
           try {
             await vault.lock(runId, ['spec.md'], 'test-design');
           } catch {
             rebased = false;
           }
           if (rebased) throw new Error('I3: an already-locked artifact was locked again, which rebases its hash onto the edited bytes');
           const after = soleTampered(await vault.verifyLocks(runId), 'a locked artifact after a refused re-lock');
           if (after.expected !== lockedSpec.sha256) {
             throw new Error(`I3: the refused re-lock still moved the manifest's hash to ${after.expected}`);
           }
         });
       },
     }),
     runtime({
       id: 'I3.locked-artifact-cannot-be-substituted',
       title:
         `a locked artifact replaced by a ${ESCAPE_MECHANISM} to identical bytes outside the artifact root verifies as tampered ` +
         `rather than being followed and hashed clean (exercised on ${process.platform} via ${ESCAPE_MECHANISM})`,
       run: async () => {
         await withVault('p1-i3-escape-', async (vault, dirs, base) => {
           const runId = 'i3-escape' as RunId;
           const inside = join(dirs.artifacts, 'sub');
           const locked = join('sub', 'spec.md');
           await mkdir(inside, { recursive: true });
           await writeFile(join(inside, 'spec.md'), '# hello\n\nThe capability, as locked.\n');
 
           const manifest = await vault.lock(runId, [locked], 'spec');
           const entry = entryFor(manifest.entries, locked);
           const before = await vault.verifyLocks(runId);
           if (!before.ok) throw new Error(`I3: the artifact verified as tampered before anything was substituted (${JSON.stringify(before.tampered)})`);
 
           // Identical bytes at the far end. Nothing but the escape
           // distinguishes this from an intact file, which is the whole point:
           // a check that compares content alone cannot see this.
           const elsewhere = join(base, 'elsewhere');
           await mkdir(elsewhere, { recursive: true });
           await writeFile(join(elsewhere, 'spec.md'), '# hello\n\nThe capability, as locked.\n');
 
           // No try/catch: a platform whose mechanism will not build fails here.
           if (ESCAPE_MECHANISM === 'junction') {
             await rm(inside, { recursive: true });
             await symlink(elsewhere, inside, 'junction');
           } else {
             await rm(join(inside, 'spec.md'));
             await symlink(join(elsewhere, 'spec.md'), join(inside, 'spec.md'));
           }
 
           // The escape is real before the Vault is asked about it, so the
           // verdict below cannot pass for the wrong reason.
           const reached = await realpath(join(dirs.artifacts, locked));
           const target = await realpath(join(elsewhere, 'spec.md'));
           if (reached !== target) {
             throw new Error(`I3: the ${ESCAPE_MECHANISM} did not take effect on ${process.platform}: ${locked} still resolves to ${reached}`);
           }
 
           const escaped = soleTampered(await vault.verifyLocks(runId), `a locked artifact substituted via a ${ESCAPE_MECHANISM}`);
           if (escaped.path !== entry.path) throw new Error(`I3: the tampered record named ${escaped.path}, expected ${entry.path}`);
           if (escaped.expected !== entry.sha256) {
             throw new Error(`I3: the tampered record's expected hash is ${escaped.expected}, not the ${entry.sha256} the manifest locked`);
           }
           if (escaped.actual !== 'escaped') {
             throw new Error(
               `I3: a locked artifact that resolves outside the artifact root reported actual '${escaped.actual}'. ` +
                 "The bytes are identical, so anything but 'escaped' means the link was followed and the substitution was hashed clean.",
             );
           }
         });
       },
     }),
     TRANSITION_REVERIFIES_LOCKS,
     STATION_LOCKS_THE_ADMITTED_ARTIFACT,
     LOCKED_PATH_IN_DIFF_IS_TAMPER,
     // P7: tamper analysis over the trees a task was handed and verified in.
     CASE_SET_REDUCTION_IS_A_DELETION,
     TAMPER_FINDING_ESCALATES_INTEGRATE,
     SKIP_MARKER_IS_A_FINDING,
     CHECK_DISPATCH_NOT_WRITABLE_BY_THE_TASK,
     // I1a: on the composed line, a task that rewrites a locked test fails the run.
     COMPOSED_HOST_LOCKED_TEST_CHANGE_FAILS,
     // R15: an acceptance check that passes on the base is a gate the change could not fail.
     PASSING_ON_THE_BASE_REFUSES,
     DEPENDENCY_TREES_ARE_THE_BASES,
+    // R29: every supported stack's tamper, coverage, and install controls, and a Python run to integrate.
+    ...R29_I3,
     // R34: every check runs over the runtime's install of the verified tree's lockfile.
     ...R34_I3,
   ],
   pending: [
     pending({
       id: 'I3.coverage-report-is-not-writable-by-the-suite',
       owner: 'M3',
       reason:
         'Changed-line coverage is read from the istanbul report a check run writes, and that run executes the '
         + "repository's own code: its tests, and whatever its package scripts do around them. So the artifact the "
         + 'coverage number is derived from is one the agent being judged can write. P8 closed the half of this that '
         + 'parsing can close — the report can no longer decide which lines count, only which of them ran, because '
         + "the denominator is now the union of the report's statements and the executable lines read from the head "
         + 'tree on the host (external review, codex-2 and gemini-2). The other half cannot be closed by reading the '
         + 'report more carefully: a hit count claiming a line ran cannot be told apart from one that did, by anyone '
         + 'who was not there when it ran. Closing it means the run that produces the report: coverage '
         + 'collected where the suite cannot rewrite it afterwards, or counted from an instrumentation the repository '
         + 'does not supply, with an assertion that a forged report does not raise the number. Until then a coverage '
         + 'figure is evidence about a suite that cooperated, not proof against one that did not. Recorded by P8 as '
         + 'D-P8-14. Owned by M3, beside mutation testing: the suite and the coverage writer share a process, so no '
         + 'location for the report decides whether its hit counts are true (D-P6-05).',
     }),
     pending({
       id: 'I3.unchanged-assertion-still-judges',
       owner: 'M3',
       reason:
         'Tamper analysis reads the test files that changed and compares what they say. An assertion it reads as '
         + 'unchanged can stop judging without its file changing, or while the change reads as nothing: a helper, fixture, '
         + 'custom matcher, setup file, or type the test imports is rewritten to pass everything; the assertion is moved '
         + 'into a branch that never runs or a function never called; a script the pinned command runs imports a module '
         + 'that now does nothing. None of those is a test file, and reachability is not decidable by parsing. The '
         + "policy's protected paths can name the files a repository knows to be load-bearing, and a pinned command's "
         + 'own script is protected (D-P7-10), but no reading of the diff decides what an unchanged assertion now '
         + 'executes. Closing it means running the suite against mutants of the code under test and requiring the '
         + 'suite that passed at the base to kill the ones it killed there, with an assertion that a helper rewritten to '
         + 'return early, and an assertion made unreachable, each lower the kill rate and escalate. Raised by the P7 '
         + 'external review (codex-1, gemini-4, gemini-5) and recorded as D-P7-11. Owned by M3, beside mutation testing.',
     }),
     pending({
       id: 'I3.runner-process-loads-only-base-code',
       owner: 'M3',
       reason:
         "A suite check runs the base's installed test framework, and nothing a task writes under node_modules is "
         + "carried (D-R15-05), but the runner's own process can still run code the change can reach. The installed "
         + 'runner is not authenticated: admission checks only that its file exists, and the suite count comes from '
         + "the adapter's static selection model, not from the process that exited, so a fabricated launcher that "
         + 'exits on a flag file fails on the base and passes at verify with no test run (codex-1). And an honest '
         + "vitest loads files outside node_modules into its own process: a globalSetup file the base's config "
         + 'already names, rewritten to call process.exit(0), makes vitest 4.1.11 exit 0 with no test run, and it is '
         + 'carried unflagged unless policy protects it (codex-2). Both were raised by the external review of the R15 '
         + 'fixes and recorded as D-R15-08. Closing it means a runner, and every input its process loads, that the '
         + 'change cannot write: provisioned by the runtime outside the tree, or authenticated and held to the base. '
         + 'That needs an assertion that each route fails the check. Tracked as issue #42. Owned by M3, beside the '
         + 'L3 canary, which needs the same trusted runner.',
     }),
   ],

         'resolvePolicy refuses a value that does not validate as a PolicyDocument, even though the parameter type says it is one, so a caller that asserts past the compiler cannot resolve a grant out of a malformed document; a genuine document still resolves',
       run: async () => {
         const { StrictPolicyEngine } = await import('@olympus-ai/core');
         const engine = new StrictPolicyEngine();
         const document = grantingDocument();
         const scope = document.roles[GRANTED_ROLE];
         if (scope === undefined) throw new Error('I5: the fixture document defines no granted role');
 
         // Each of these is what a caller produces by writing `value as

         // never authorised.
         const rogues: Array<{ readonly what: string; readonly value: unknown }> = [
           { what: 'a global cap of 99', value: { ...document, globalCap: 99 } },
           { what: 'a role ceiling of 99', value: { ...document, roles: { [GRANTED_ROLE]: { ...scope, autonomyCeiling: 99 } } } },
           { what: 'a station id that is not one of the ten', value: { ...document, roles: { [GRANTED_ROLE]: { ...scope, stations: ['deploy'] } } } },

           if (!refused) {
             throw new Error(
               `I5: resolvePolicy accepted ${what}. The parameter type is a compiler claim, and a control that holds ` +
                 'only while every caller remembers to validate first is not closed.',
             );
           }
         }
 
         // The control: a genuine document resolves. A guard that threw on
         // everything would satisfy the loop above and break the engine.
         const policy = engine.resolvePolicy(document);
         if (policy.globalCap !== document.globalCap) {
           throw new Error(`I5: a valid document resolved to globalCap ${String(policy.globalCap)}, expected ${String(document.globalCap)}`);
         }
       },
     }),
     runtime({
       id: 'I5.malformed-autonomy-level-refused',
       title:
         'resolveAutonomy refuses a requested level that is not one of the four, rather than comparing it against the cap: NaN, a negative, and a fraction all survive a numeric comparison and would otherwise be answered with success carrying a level that is not a level',
       run: async () => {
         const { AUTONOMY_LEVELS, StrictPolicyEngine } = await import('@olympus-ai/core');
         const engine = new StrictPolicyEngine();

         for (const [label, level] of [
           ['NaN', Number.NaN], ['-1', -1], ['2.5', 2.5], ['4', 4],

             const outcome = engine.resolveAutonomy(level as never, 'verify', GRANTED_ROLE, policy);
             if (outcome.ok) {
               throw new Error(

                   'An unsupported input is a refusal, and success is the one answer a caller must never get for a ' +
                   'level nobody can honour.',
               );
             }
           } catch (error) {
             if (error instanceof Error && error.message.startsWith('I5:')) throw error;
             refused = true;
           }
           if (!refused) throw new Error(`I5: resolveAutonomy returned a refusal rather than refusing ${label} outright`);
         }
 
         // The control: every real level is still answered normally.
         for (const level of AUTONOMY_LEVELS) {

           const allowed = level <= ROLE_CEILING;
           if (outcome.ok !== allowed) {
             throw new Error(`I5: level ${String(level)} at 'verify' was ${outcome.ok ? 'granted' : 'refused'}, expected the opposite`);
           }
         }
       },
     }),
     runtime({
       id: 'I5.sandbox-egress-allowlist-enforced',
       title:
         'a sandbox provisioned with egress mode allowlist reaches the one host it names and no other, by name and by address; ' +
         'the direct route does not exist, so unsetting every proxy variable reaches nothing; an empty allow list is refused rather ' +
         'than read as deny-all or allow-all; and deny-all is unchanged, with --network none, no proxy, and no network created',
       run: async () => {
         await withProvider('i5-egress-allowlist-', async (provider, dirs) => {
           // An allowlist that names nothing is refused. It is not deny-all wearing the wrong
           // name, and it is not permission to reach everything; either reading would be the
           // silent degrade this invariant exists to refuse (D-P2-07, reversed by P10).
           const empty = await refusalFrom(() => provider.provision(specFor(dirs, 'rw', { egress: { mode: 'allowlist', allow: [] } })));
           if (empty.layer !== 'egress') {
             throw new Error(`I5: an empty allowlist was refused at the ${empty.layer} layer, not the egress layer`);
           }
 
           // deny-all is untouched by the allowlist existing: no proxy is started beside a
           // sandbox entitled to no egress, and no network is created for it.
           await withSandbox(provider, specFor(dirs), async (handle) => {
             const controls = provider.appliedControls(handle);
             if (controls.egress.mode !== 'deny-all') {
               throw new Error(`I5: a deny-all spec was applied as ${controls.egress.mode}`);
             }
             // `network: 'none'` is the type's guarantee once the mode is deny-all, so what is
             // read here is what Docker was actually told and what the container actually got.
             if (!controls.runArgs.join(' ').includes('--network none')) {
               throw new Error(`I5: a deny-all sandbox was not started with --network none: ${controls.runArgs.join(' ')}`);
             }
             const interfaces = await provider.exec(handle, ['ls', '/sys/class/net']);
             if (interfaces.stdout.trim().split(/\s+/).join(',') !== 'lo') {
               throw new Error(`I5: a deny-all sandbox has interfaces [${interfaces.stdout.trim()}]; expected loopback alone`);
             }
           });
 
           const name = originName();
           const handle = await provider.provision(specFor(dirs, 'rw', { egress: { mode: 'allowlist', allow: [name] } }));
           const { egress } = allowlistControls(provider, handle);
           try {
             await withOrigin(egress.proxy.outboundNetwork, name, async (origin) => {
               // The grant. The body proves the origin answered, not the proxy on its behalf.
               const reached = await provider.exec(handle, [
                 'sh', '-c', `wget -T 8 -O - http://${origin.name}:${String(ORIGIN_PORT)}/ 2>&1`,
               ]);
               if (!reached.stdout.includes(ORIGIN_BODY)) {
                 throw new Error(`I5: the allowlisted host was not reached: ${reached.stdout}${reached.stderr}`);
               }
 
               // Refused by name, and by address. The second is the one that matters: a refusal
               // that only ever fires on a name is a DNS failure wearing an allowlist's clothes.
               for (const host of [BLOCKED_NAME, BLOCKED_ADDRESS]) {
                 const answer = await throughProxy(provider, handle, `GET http://${host}/ HTTP/1.0`);
                 if (!answer.includes('403') || !answer.includes(host)) {
                   throw new Error(`I5: ${host} is not on the allowlist and was not refused by name in the answer: ${answer}`);
                 }
               }
 
               // The strongest form: the origin's own address, which the proxy demonstrably
               // reaches, refused for the one reason under test — the address is not on the list.
               const sameHost = await throughProxy(provider, handle, `GET http://${origin.address}:${String(ORIGIN_PORT)}/ HTTP/1.0`);
               if (!sameHost.includes('403') || sameHost.includes(ORIGIN_BODY)) {
                 throw new Error(`I5: a reachable host was allowed by address although only its name was granted: ${sameHost}`);
               }
 
               // There is nothing to bypass. The internal network carries no default route, so a
               // process that unsets every proxy variable is left with no second path to take.
               const routes = await provider.exec(handle, ['sh', '-c', 'ip route']);
               if (routes.stdout.includes('default')) {
                 throw new Error(`I5: the sandbox has a default route, so the proxy is a convention rather than the only way out: ${routes.stdout}`);
               }
               const bypass = await provider.exec(handle, [
                 'sh', '-c',
                 'unset http_proxy https_proxy HTTP_PROXY HTTPS_PROXY no_proxy NO_PROXY; ' +
                   `wget -T 4 -O - http://${origin.address}:${String(ORIGIN_PORT)}/ 2>&1`,
               ]);
               if (bypass.exitCode === 0 || bypass.stdout.includes(ORIGIN_BODY)) {
                 throw new Error(`I5: the allowlisted host was reached with every proxy variable unset: ${bypass.stdout}`);
               }
             });
           } finally {
             await provider.destroy(handle);
           }
 
           // The proxy is the sandbox's, and goes with it. One left running is a route off the
           // host with no workload behind it and nobody watching it.
           for (const [kind, leaked] of [
             ['container', egress.proxy.name],
             ['network', egress.proxy.internalNetwork],
             ['network', egress.proxy.outboundNetwork],
           ] as const) {
             if (await dockerHas(kind, leaked)) throw new Error(`I5: the ${kind} ${leaked} outlived the sandbox it served`);
           }
         });
       },
     }),
     STATION_MISSING_CAPABILITY_REFUSED,
     TASK_ATTEMPTS_ARE_BOUNDED,
     OVER_REQUEST_REFUSED_AT_ADMISSION,
     UNSUPPORTED_STACK_IS_LOUD,
